| Method | Path | Capability / auth | Listener | Phase | Replaces | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| GET | `/v1/hubs` | authenticated | internal | P1 | 11 inline reads + `taskService.fetchHubs`: `taskService.ts:20`, `job-assign/page.tsx:119`, `first-mile`/`line-haul :98`, `income:297`, `rate-card:392`, `sources:141`, `billing.ts:747,1181`, `useDriverMonitor:506`; server `fn:lineNotify.ts:81`, `fn:tripBillingOnDelivered.ts:125` | **Single DTO** (W8): `{id, sourceId, sourceNameTh, sourceNameEn, latitude, longitude, stationType, linkedCustomerId, linkedCustomerName, linkedCustomerKind, createdByDriver}`; pages adapt with TanStack `select` (replaces 4 shapes); `?stationType&q`; ETag; Redis `cache:hubs:all`; `['hubs']` 10 min; new `features/hubs/api` on the web |
| GET | `/v1/hubs/maps` | authenticated | internal | P1 | `buildHubMaps` (`fn:tripBillingOnDelivered.ts:124-141`), `web:lib/placeFilter.ts`, `hubDisplay.ts` | `{nameToCode:{...}, codeToName:{...}}` as two separate objects backed by two Redis keys (never merged, `.vibe-rules.md:2379`); display/filter use only — billing resolves codes from PostgreSQL inside the pricing transaction |
| POST | `/v1/hubs` | `operations:manage_sources` | internal | P1 | `hub-dialog.tsx:168` | hubSchema (`sourceNameTh` required, `sourceNameEn` optional); `UNIQUE (source_id)` -> `409`; outbox `hubs.changed` (cache delete + SSE). Driver-created hubs: `POST /v1/mobile/hubs` |
| PATCH | `/v1/hubs/{id}` | `operations:manage_sources` | internal | P1 | `hub-dialog.tsx:166` | Partial; `hubs.changed` |
| POST | `/v1/hubs/import` | `operations:manage_sources` | internal | P1 | `pickup-import-dialog.tsx:310-347`, `scripts/import-hubs.ts` | XLSX/JSON rows upserted by `source_id`; `hubs.changed` |
| GET | `/v1/distances` | authenticated | internal | P1 | `HubDistancePanel.tsx:30` | `?hubId=` -> rows of `hub_soc_distances` (one table with `direction`, R14) |
| GET | `/v1/distances/{originId}/{destinationId}` | authenticated | internal | P1 | `mob:hub_soc_distances_repository.dart:50-80` (staff copy) | `{distanceKm, durationMinutes, direction}`; direction derived from station types |
| GET | `/v1/distances/meta` | authenticated | internal | P1 | `metadata/distances_last_calculated` (`sources/page.tsx:169`) | `{lastCalculatedAt}` from `settings` |
| POST | `/v1/jobs/distances.compute` | `operations:calculate_distances` | internal | P1 | `computeHubSocDistances` (`fn:distances.ts:243`; caller `sources/page.tsx:281`) | `202 {jobId}`; queue `distances.compute`; duplicate guard `lock:job:distances.compute:all`; at most 4 runs a Bangkok month (`409 failed_precondition`, `monthly_run_limit`; platform admin exempt, T21); SPX/SPK network grouping port of `fn:core/distances.ts`; stale pairs kept |

**As built (T21, issue #41).** `internal/hubs` serves these routes on the internal listener behind `auth.RequireAuth`; requests run in `db.WithPrincipal` (RLS global-read-steward-write, plus the driver hub insert), and the steward keys `operations:manage_sources` / `operations:calculate_distances` are only ever effective for own-fleet staff or a platform admin acting as a tenant (`X-Act-On-Tenant: <uuid>`): a carrier holding either key by override gets `403 permission_denied`, and RLS refuses its write (`42501`) even without the guard (R60). **DTO:** `GET /v1/hubs` answers the whole master ordered by code (a few hundred rows, no pagination) as `{id, sourceId, sourceNameTh, sourceNameEn, latitude, longitude, stationType, network, linkedPartyId, linkedCustomerId, linkedCustomerName, linkedCustomerKind, createdByDriver, updatedAt}` (`network`, `linkedPartyId`, `updatedAt` additive): `linkedCustomerId` is the customer (`linkedCustomerKind: customer`) or carrier tenant (`partner`) the hub's billing party bills, `linkedCustomerName` the legacy denormalised `CODE — Name`; `?stationType` (HUB \| SOC, legacy spellings accepted) and `?q` (code or names) filter in memory. `cache:hubs:all`, `cache:hubs:n2c` and `cache:hubs:c2n` are loaded in a READ ONLY `db.WithSystem` transaction so every reader caches the same value (carrier names are not readable by every principal), and each response masks `linkedCustomerId` / `linkedCustomerName` where the caller's RLS would not read that row, so the list equals the RLS-backed read of each hub (a customer-scope principal sees only its own parties, of any kind, 0012, and for any other party also no `linkedCustomerKind`, since RLS hides the party row itself; carrier staff see another carrier's name only in their reach; `linkedPartyId` is a column of the global-read `hubs` and is never masked; `internal/hubs` joins the `WithSystem` allow-list of Appendix C §C.3.2 for these loads and the job). `GET /v1/hubs` and `/v1/hubs/maps` send a strong `ETag` (sha256 of the body), `Cache-Control: private, no-cache`, and answer `304` to a matching `If-None-Match`. **Maps:** `nameToCode` comes from `hub_name_aliases` and leaves out every alias that is itself a hub code, so a destination already stored as a code (`SPK890174`) resolves to itself and never into another hub or back into a name; the writes below keep that true of the stored data (invariant 8, which pricing's `compute.NewHubMaps` over `hubs.name_th` / `name_en` + `hub_name_aliases` also relies on, §6 "Hub maps"), so this read filter is defence in depth for legacy data loaded by the ETL; a hub's own name aliases carry its current spelling (a rename that changes only the case rewrites the `citext` key's spelling, so the current name stays a key); `codeToName` is `hubs.name_th`; two Redis hashes, never merged. **Writes:** `sourceId` is stored trimmed and upper case (`UNIQUE (source_id)` on `citext`: `SPK-gw` and `SPK-GW` collide, `409 already_exists` with `details.field = sourceId`); `sourceNameTh` required, coordinates both or neither (`422 invalid_argument`); the link is `linkedPartyId`, or the legacy pair `linkedCustomerId` + `linkedCustomerKind` (`customer` \| `partner`), `null` unlinks; `network` is recomputed on every write from the code and the linked party's code (`hubDistanceNetworkGroup`). **Name rule (invariant 8, developer-spec §17 item 8):** a name a write sets (create, a changed name on `PATCH` or import, the driver's `sourceName`) may not be another hub's code, case-insensitively: `422 invalid_argument` with reason `is_hub_code` on `sourceNameTh` / `sourceNameEn` / `sourceName` (`rows[i].<field>` on import); a new or renamed code may not be another hub's alias or Thai or English name: `409 already_exists` with `details.field = sourceId` (`rows[i].sourceId` on import) and `details.reason = taken_as_name` (a code that is another hub's code stays `reason = taken`); a name equal to the hub's own code is allowed (it is never an alias), a legacy name a write does not change is not checked again (and never stored as an alias), and a renamed hub drops its own aliases equal to its new code. Every hub write takes the transaction advisory lock `hubs:names` first, so two writes cannot cross the rule. Each write keeps `hub_name_aliases` equal to the hub's names (blank and own-code names skipped, the first writer of a name keeps it; a name a hub gives up goes to the oldest hub still carrying it), appends `hubs.changed {ids}` (SSE `global`) and drops the three hub keys after COMMIT; a `PATCH` that changes nothing writes nothing. `POST /v1/hubs/import` takes `{rows: [{sourceId, sourceNameTh?, sourceNameEn?, latitude?, longitude?, stationType?, linkedPartyId? \| linkedCustomerId? + linkedCustomerKind?}]}` (at most 2000; the web parses the XLSX, as today): an existing code takes the fields its row carries (absent, `null` or blank keeps the value: a workbook's empty cell arrives as `""`; import never clears, clearing is a `PATCH`), a new one needs a name (`sourceNameTh`, else `sourceNameEn`, the legacy organic rule) and defaults to `HUB`; any invalid row or a code twice refuses the whole import (`422`, `details.fields[].field = rows[i].<field>`); the answer is `{created, updated, unchanged, items[{index, id, sourceId, outcome}]}` and one `hubs.changed` carries every changed id. The driver's hub (`mobile:create_hub` + `driver:self`) is `hubs.Service.CreateDriverHub` (`{sourceId, sourceName, stationType?}`, the APK's `addHubByDriver` subset; `created_by_driver = true`, name in both columns as the APK stores it); coordinates are steward-only (a body that sends `latitude` / `longitude` has them ignored), so a driver hub enters the Distance Matrix once a steward sets them with `PATCH /v1/hubs/{id}`, as in the legacy app; its route `POST /v1/mobile/hubs` is mounted with the mobile group at P7a. **Distances:** `GET /v1/distances?hubId&direction&limit&cursor` (keyset on `id`) lists `{id, hubId, hubSourceId, socKey, direction, network, distanceMeters, distanceKm, durationSeconds, durationMinutes, hubLat, hubLng, socLat, socLng, updatedAt}`; `hubId` is a uuid or a code, and a SOC's id lists the rows of its key; `GET /v1/distances/{originId}/{destinationId}` takes uuids or codes (a SOC also by its key, e.g. `SOCE`; the path segments are percent-decoded, so a code with a space or Thai text such as `SOCE%20(...)` or `SPK890103-%E0...` resolves as in `?hubId`, and a malformed escape is `404`) and answers `404` for HUB -> HUB, SOC -> SOC, an unknown point or a pair never computed; `GET /v1/distances/meta` is `{lastCalculatedAt, pairs?}` (`null` before the first run; `cache:settings:distances_last_calculated`). **Job:** `POST /v1/jobs/distances.compute` (no body) submits the job owned by the caller and filed under its effective tenant, so that tenant's staff holding `operations:calculate_distances` read it (`jobs.TypeCapabilities`, §B.2.20); a second submission while one runs is `409 already_exists` with `details.jobId`, and a fifth run in a Bangkok month (queued, running and succeeded runs count, and so does a failed run that completed at least one billed request, `progress.done > 0`; a run refused on its first request is free) is `409 failed_precondition` with `details.reason = monthly_run_limit`, `limit`, `runs` (the 4 runs a month of the `.vibe-rules.md` Hub-SOC Distance section, as of commit 4f552099), except for a platform admin. The worker's consumer (queue `distances.compute`) reads the hubs and SOCs with coordinates in legacy document order (SOC codes starting with `0` skipped, one SOC per network and key), plans per network exactly as `computeRowsForNetwork` (HUB -> SOC: every SOC per request, `min(25, 100 / socs)` hubs; SOC -> HUB: `max(1, 100 / min(hubs, 25))` SOCs against up to 25 hubs; more than 25 SOCs in a network is a warning and that network is skipped), never pairs SPX with SPK, spaces requests 200 ms apart, reports `{done, total}` per request, then writes every pair of both directions (`ON CONFLICT (hub_id, soc_key, direction)`, `distance_km` / `duration_min` rounded as `Math.round(x * 100) / 100`; pairs not computed stay), `settings['distances_last_calculated'] = {at, pairs, hubs, socs, jobId}`, outbox `settings.changed {key}` (cache only, no realtime topic) and the job's success `{written, hubsCount, socsCount, requests, calculatedAt, networks, warnings?}` in one transaction, and releases the lock. With `DISTANCE_MATRIX_ENABLED` off (locally), an API refusal (`Distance Matrix API: <status>`, the key never in a message), no SOC, no hub or no pair, the job ends `failed` with that text and writes nothing (a run that failed after billed requests records `{requests, hubsCount, socsCount}` as the failed job's result). The client (`internal/platform/gmaps`) is an interface; tests use an in-process fake, so no test calls Google.

| POST | `/v1/companies` | `company:manage` (active tenant) | internal | P1 | `companies.ts:66-104` | companySchema incl. `withholdingTaxRate` (required, no default; snapshotted onto each statement, R18) and `logoKey`, `stampKey`, `signatureKey` (private objects, read server-side by the PDF renderer; `GET /v1/files` needs `company:view`); `companyType: owner` only in the own fleet (`422` `owner_own_fleet_only`), one owner (`409 already_exists`); outbox `companies.changed` (SSE `global`, Firestore projection P1-P5, R71) |

**As built (T20, issue #40).** `internal/parties` serves `/v1/customers*` and `/v1/companies*` in `db.WithPrincipal` (RLS decides, and the list queries still name the reach, main spec §4.7, Appendix C §C.3.8: the company list and owner reads pass `all_tenants` / `tenant_ids` from `authz.Principal.TenantReach`, the effective tenant of a staff principal plus its contractor reach, which is exactly what `p_tenant_staff` applies; the customer list passes a customer-scope principal's parties); `internal/iam` serves `/v1/subcontractors*` next to the T19 tenant routes (reads in `db.WithPrincipal`, writes in `db.WithSystem` after Go authorization, since no RLS policy lets a steward write another tenant's profile). Customers: `code` is stored upper case (`^[A-Z0-9][A-Z0-9_-]{0,31}$`, unique case-insensitively, `409 already_exists` `field: code`); `taxId`, a company's `taxId` and a carrier's `idCardNumber` / `taxId` pass the 13-digit Thai checksum (`422 invalid_argument`, `details.fields[]`); `branchType` is `hq` \| `branch` for customers and `headquarters` \| `branch` for companies, a branch needs its 5-digit `branchNumber`; `driverIdTypes[]` (at most 20, unique `key`) replaces the set on `PATCH`; `billingDateBasis` lives on the customer's billing party; a customer-scope principal's minimal list holds only its own parties (and RLS narrows `customers`, `billing_parties`, `customer_driver_id_types` for it, migration `0012`, Appendix C §C.3.5); `PATCH` with no change writes nothing. Files: `logoKey`, `stampKey`, `signatureKey` (and a carrier's `documentKeys`, purpose `tenant_document`) are keys of `POST /v1/uploads/presign`, committed in the entity transaction (main spec §9.4); payloads carry `…Key` and a presigned `…Url`; `null` unlinks a file (its object stays, main spec §9.3). A `PATCH` may send the loaded record back: a key the entity already links in that slot stays linked without a new commit, whoever uploaded it (the rule of a carrier's `documentKeys`), so an editor who is neither the uploader nor in its tenant (own-fleet staff on a carrier's company through contractor reach, a platform admin acting as another tenant, a logo the ETL registered) can save; only a new key must be an upload of the caller or of its tenant (`422` `not_found` otherwise). `POST /v1/subcontractors` follows the legacy wizard rule (an individual needs `idCardNumber`, a company `taxId`) and answers the profile; carriers are onboarded from the own fleet (`fleet:manage_subcontractors` with the own fleet active) or by `platform:manage_tenants`, both with `contractorTenantId` = the own fleet (`409 failed_precondition` `no_own_fleet` without one); API keys are refused on the carrier-profile writes (audit rows name a user).

| Method | Path | Capability / auth | Listener | Phase | Replaces | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| GET | `/v1/tasks` | `operations:view_first_mile` \| `operations:view_line_haul` \| `dispatch:view_operations` | internal | P2 | listeners `first-mile/page.tsx:171-182`, `line-haul:171-182`, `job-assign:179-186`, `useDriverMonitor.ts:643-661`; `taskService.ts:51-84`; `documentId in` chunks `billing.ts:851`, `useDriverMonitor:678-697`, `income:453` | `?taskType&date&from&to&status[]&driverId&truckId&q&cursor`; sort `plan_date DESC` with a date filter else `created_at DESC`; customer scope and dispatcher projection by RLS and the `scope_tasks` view; `['tasks',{date,type,driverId,status}]` 30 s + `keepPreviousData` |
| GET | `/v1/tasks/{id}` | `operations:view_first_mile` \| `operations:view_line_haul` \| `driver:self` | internal | P2 | `EditTripDetailsDialog.tsx:138-144` | Task with stops, check-in fields, `tripId`; `driver:self` allowed per R33 (driver app reaches the same service at `GET /v1/mobile/tasks/{id}`) |

Server: `task_no` from the SECURITY DEFINER allocator `next_task_seq()` over `task_number_counters (task_type, plan_date)`, **global across tenants** (R10, R66), format `FM\|LH-ddMMyyyy-NNN`; `run_order = max+1` per driver in tx under the per-driver advisory lock `operationsdb.LockDriverQueue` (T27; the `PATCH /v1/tasks/{id}` assignment takes the same lock, never a `drivers` row lock); driver/plate/linked-customer snapshots from master tables; vehicle class from the mapper, unknown -> `NULL` (R15); status `assigned\|pending`; tenant = driver's tenant, else caller's tenant with `tenant_source='form'` (R13). Outbox `task.created`, `task.assigned` -> `notify.fcm`. Firestore write-back until P7b (main spec §13) |
| PATCH | `/v1/tasks/{id}` | `operations:manage_tasks` \| `platform:cross_tenant_write` (the R13 reassignment of a platform admin without `X-Act-On-Tenant`) | internal | P2 | `updateDoc` `useFirstMileTask.ts:384`, `income/page.tsx:227`, `EditTripDetailsDialog.tsx:251`, `billing.ts:468` | Partial; `jobCategory` absent = unchanged; status is not rewritten unless `driverId` changes (fixes `fn:tasks.ts:120-137`); helper change checks payroll lock (`409 payroll_locked`); `date` change on a delivered trip emits `task.plan_date_changed` -> billing restamp under the period lock; `tenantId` accepted only from dispatcher/platform, `task_tenant_reassigned` in tx (R13). Outbox `task.updated`, `task.reassigned` (FCM unassigned/assigned). As built (T28): the reassignment slice only, below |
| POST | `/v1/tasks/{id}/cancel` | `operations:manage_tasks` | internal | P2 | `first-mile/page.tsx:243`, `line-haul:243`, `job-assign:306` | `{reason?}`; outbox `task.cancelled` -> FCM `_task_cancelled` |
| POST | `/v1/tasks/import` | `operations:manage_tasks` | internal | P2 | `first-mile/import-dialog.tsx:304-355`, `line-haul:310-346`, `job-assign:312-350` | XLSX/JSON -> `{created[], errors[]}`; numbers sequenced server-side; plates resolved to fleet (unknown plate = row rejected, blank plate allowed); `notify=false` default |
| POST | `/v1/tasks/{id}/stops` | `operations:manage_tasks` | internal | P2 | `addDeliveryStop` (`fn:multiDeliveryTrips.ts:341`) staff use | `{destination, sourceId?, isCustom?, destinationLinkedPartyId?, destinationCustomerLinkKind?}` -> stops[]; bootstraps stop 1 from `task.destination`; `409 duplicate_destination`; driver path `POST /v1/mobile/tasks/{id}/stops` |
| POST | `/v1/tasks/{id}/notify-line` | `operations:edit_trip_details` | internal | P5 | force path of `sendCustomerLineNotification` (`fn:lineNotify.ts:259`, any user could force today) | `{force?}` -> `202 {jobId, status}`; publishes `job.notify.line.force` (check-in card). As built (T49): the task in the caller's write reach (`FOR UPDATE` under RLS, else `404`); `409 already_exists` (`details.jobId`) while a job for the same card waits; an API key or `X-Act-On-Tenant: *` `403` |
| GET | `/v1/tasks/{id}/trip` | `operations:view_first_mile` \| `operations:view_line_haul` | internal | P2 | `first-mile/page.tsx:136-141`, `job-assign:154` | Trip or `404` |

**As built (T28, issue #48): the R13 reassignment slice of `PATCH /v1/tasks/{id}`.** Until T31 lands the rest of the route, the body is `{tenantId, driverId?}`: `tenantId` is required (a uuid), `driverId` a uuid or `null` (leave the task without a driver), and every other field is `422 invalid_argument` `not_supported` (refused, never dropped); a query parameter is `400`. Only a dispatcher holding `operations:manage_tasks` or a `platform_admin` holding `platform:cross_tenant_write` may send it (`403 permission_denied` `details.reason=dispatcher_or_platform_only`; an API key never). A dispatcher must read the task (otherwise `404`) and its own RLS must let it update the task: the task's tenant is its organisation's tenant or in that tenant's contractor reach (`authz.Principal.TenantReach`, the reach of `p_tenant_staff`), i.e. a call-out it has not handed over yet. Another carrier's task that it reads only through its parties' scope is `403 permission_denied` `details.reason=task_not_in_reach` whatever the body, so the dispatcher never changes another carrier's task, neither its tenant nor its driver (Appendix C §C.9.2 #12); a call-out handed to a carrier is then that carrier's (whether a dispatcher may take one back is an owner question on #48). The path is strictly the R13 move: `tenantId` equal to the task's tenant with a driver change is `422 tenantId same_tenant` (a driver change without a move is T31's ordinary update under the caller's RLS, where a platform admin needs `X-Act-On-Tenant`), and a request that changes nothing answers `200` with `moved: false` and writes nothing. `iam.TaskMover` runs one system transaction (main spec §4.7): the task row locked (`409 failed_precondition task_changed` when a dispatcher's view moved meanwhile; a quarantined task is `404`, it moves only through the re-home), the target existing and not the quarantine tenant (`422 tenantId not_found`) and active (`409 tenant_inactive`), the task `pending` or `assigned` (`409 task_not_open`) and without trip records (`409 task_has_trips`); a new driver of the target tenant only (`422 driverId not_found` / `not_in_tenant` / `same_as_helper`, `409 driver_inactive`), the current driver kept only when it belongs to the target (`409 driver_not_in_tenant`), the helper and the truck kept only when their tenant is the target or linked to it by `contractor_tenant_id` either way (`409 helper_not_in_tenant`, `409 truck_not_in_tenant`). `tenancy.MoveTask` moves the task (`tenant_source='form'`) with its standby records (`'task'`) and the files they own under `app.tenant_move` for that move only; a driver change sets `pending` <-> `assigned` and the end of the driver's queue under `LockDriverQueue`, clearing the legacy driver reference (a kept driver keeps its status and queue position); `task_tenant_reassigned` (warning, actor the caller, `details {taskId, taskNo, from, to, driverId, previousDriverId, by, standbyRecords, files}`) is appended twice with the same details, `tenant_id` the target and the source, so the staff of the tenant that now runs the task and of the one that lost it both read the move; outbox `task.reassigned` (`{id, driverId, previousDriverId, ...}` for notify.fcm) when the driver changed, else `task.updated` with `changed: ["tenantId"]` (a move that keeps the driver: the silent `tasks_changed`, never a second "assigned" push), on `tenant:{to}:tasks`, `tenant:{from}:tasks`, `dispatch:{party}:tasks` of every party of the task and `driver:{id}` of the drivers concerned. The answer is `200 {data: {id, taskNo, tenantId, tenantSource, previousTenantId, status, driverId, previousDriverId, runOrder, moved}}` (`previousDriverId` only when the driver changed).

**As built (T30, issue #50): the four task reads.** `GET /v1/tasks` takes `?taskType&date|from&to&status&driverId&truckId&q&limit&cursor` (`date` and `from`/`to` are Bangkok plan days, both inclusive, and exclusive of each other; `status` repeatable or comma-separated, canonical lower_snake); a plan-day range lists on `(plan_date, id)`, latest day first, otherwise `(created_at, id)`, newest first; keyset `?limit` 1-500 (default 50), no cap. The task type is checked against the caller: `operations:view_first_mile` reads first-mile tasks, `operations:view_line_haul` line-haul ones, `dispatch:view_operations` both; a `taskType` the caller does not read is `403 permission_denied` (`details.missingCapability`), and a one-type reader without `taskType` lists its type; the same rule answers `403` on `GET /v1/tasks/{id}` and drops the other type from `by-number`. Each row carries its planned stops (`stops[]`, stop 1 the task's destination), its newest trip (`trip: {id, tripNo, status}`), the driver's and helper's display names (`driverName`, `helperName`: Thai full name, else first and last name) and the resolved places (`source`, `destinationPlace`: `{raw, key, label, resolved}`, the place rule of the monitor below); staff rows add `tenantSource`, `billingPartyId`, `sourceLinkedPartyId`, `destinationLinkedPartyId`, `legacyTruckType`, `cancelledAt`, `cancelReason` and each stop's `destinationLinkedPartyId`. A dispatcher or a customer-scope principal reads `scope_tasks` and `scope_task_stops` (0017) only, without those fields. `GET /v1/tasks/{id}` admits a driver acting as itself (`driver:self`, R33: its own or helper task, without the party links; another driver's task is `404`) and signs the check-in photo and app screenshot (`checkInPhoto`, `checkInAppScreenshot`: `{fileId, key, url}`, 15 min). As built (T34, issue #54): `GET /v1/tasks` also takes `sourceHub` and `destination`, the boards' source-hub and SOC filters (W9: a filter is in the request, never over the loaded rows): each is a place filter value of the monitor's vocabulary (`code:<CODE>` the hubs of that code and, for `SOCE|SOCN|SOCW`, the SOC key; `hub:<uuid>`; `raw:<text>`; `__none__`; absent or `all`: no filter; anything else `422 sourceHub|destination invalid`) matched on the task's own source and destination place (`source.key`, `destinationPlace.key`: the hub stored at write, else the SOC key stored or of the raw text, else the trimmed raw text), on both axes, in the staff query and over `scope_tasks` alike. The board sends `destination=code:SOCN` and `sourceHub=code:<hub code>`. `GET /v1/tasks/by-number/{taskNo}` (percent-decoded) returns every match of the caller's reach, oldest first. `GET /v1/tasks/{id}/trip` answers the task's newest trip exactly as `GET /v1/trips/{id}`, or `404` when the task has none. The driver's queue service of `GET /v1/mobile/tasks` (P7a, T57) is `operations.Service.DriverQueue`: own and helper tasks in the queue order of ADR 0028 (`compareTasksForDriverQueue`, `task_repository.dart:16-70`), computed in SQL before the limit: the dispatch instant `queueAt` (`actual_pickup_at`, else the Bangkok plan day at `plan_time`, 00:00 without one), then `run_order` with the tasks that have none first, then `created_at`, then `id`. Without `status[]` it is the open queue (`pending`, `assigned`, `checked_in`, `in_transit`: the tasks the check-in gate walks; completed and cancelled ones never block it); `status[]` (history included) reads those statuses in the same order. A keyset page on that key, `limit` 1-500 (default 500): a longer queue gets `nextCursor`, never a silent cut (TW8); each item is the task with its `queueAt`. The route ships with T57.

**As built (T31, issue #51): the task writes** (`POST /v1/tasks`, `PATCH /v1/tasks/{id}`, `POST /v1/tasks/{id}/cancel`, `POST /v1/tasks/{id}/stops`, `POST /v1/tasks/import`) are described with the trip, standby and incident writes after §B.2.13. The T28 slice of `PATCH /v1/tasks/{id}` above stays the path of a body whose `tenantId` differs from the task's tenant (or names a task the caller does not read); every other body is the ordinary update, so a `tenantId` equal to the task's tenant with a driver change is no longer `422 tenantId same_tenant` but that update.

**As built (T30, issue #50): the trip and incident reads and the monitor.** Common rules: `from` / `to` are a Bangkok day `yyyy-MM-dd` (the day included) or an RFC 3339 instant (`[from, to)`); keyset pages with opaque cursors bound to their axis (`?limit` 1-500, default 50; the monitor 1-200, default 200); an undocumented parameter is `400`; a dispatcher or a customer-scope principal reads the `scope_*` projections only (`scope_trips`, `scope_trip_stops`, `scope_trip_photos`, `scope_incidents`, `scope_drivers`, Appendix C §C.3.7), so its payloads carry no billing, party, price, review, OCR, evidence, stamping or HR field. Photos are signed after the referencing row was read under the caller's rights, for 15 minutes.

- `GET /v1/trips`: `axis=created` (default, `status` allowed) | `delivered` | `billing` (Bangkok days, both inclusive); `customerId` is the billed party. Staff rows add `tenantSource`, `truckType` (raw), `distanceKm`, `sealTime`, `durationMinutes`, `deliveredVia`, `needsAdminReview`, `reviewStatus`, `reviewReason`, `resubmittedAt`, `ocrData` and, when `billing.IncomeBillingVisible` (`accounting:view_income`, never a dispatcher), the `billing` block: `billing.TripBillingOf`'s projection read in a request transaction of the caller (§B.2.14 as built, T39: `{billingPartyId, customerId, billingDate, priced, estimateThb, unpricedReason, baseRateThb, stopChargeThb, rateEntryId, rateImportId, lookupHubCode, lookupDestinationCode, fuelAdjustmentId, rateMultiplier, addThbPerTrip, fuelEffectiveFromDate, roundEffectiveFromDate, fuelBandLowerThb, fuelBandUpperThb, referenceFuelPriceThb, isMultiDelivery, manualOverride, jobCategoryAtPricing, computedBy, computedAt, stops[{stopIndex, destinationCode, baseRateThb, finalRateThb}]}`, every price field null without a snapshot the caller may read, R61) plus `billingAxisDate`: the fields the Income page and its Excel export read (`income/page.tsx:339-379, 730-734`). `GET /v1/trips/{id}` and `/by-no` carry the same block. A scope principal's `axis=billing` and `customerId` are `422 not_available`.
- `GET /v1/trips/{id}` and `/by-no/{tripNo}` (percent-decoded): the trip with `stops[]`, `photos[]` (ADR 0018 rank) and, for staff, `renames[]`; by number falls back to the most recent pre-rename number with `matchedPreviousNo: true` (staff only: `trip_no_history` is staff-only, so a scope principal resolves current numbers only). `GET /v1/trips/{id}/photos` answers `[{id, type, known, stopId, fileId, key, url, geocoding: {lat, lng, address, at}, rank, createdAt}]` with `Cache-Control: private, no-store`.
- `GET /v1/trips/monitor`: `?from&to` (or `?month=yyyy-MM`, a Bangkok month) required, at most 366 days; `status` (`incident` also matches a trip with an incident report), `jobType`, `driverId`, `customerId` (staff), `partner` (a code or `__none__`), `plate` (`id:<truckId>` | `plate:<raw plate>` | `__none__`), `vehicleClass` (a class or `__none__`), `origin`, `destination` (`code:<CODE>` | `raw:<text>` | `__none__`; a multi-drop trip matches by any of its stops), `q` (a substring of the trip or task number or the driver's name), `limit` 1-200 (default 200), `cursor`, `facets=false`. Pages newest first on `created_at` (the loading time), keyset `(created_at, id)`; every filter runs in SQL. A row is `{id, tenantId, tripNo, status, jobType, jobCategory, createdAt, updatedAt, deliveredAt, taskId, taskNo, taskStatus, checkInAt, runOrder, planAt, driverId, driverName, origin, destination, stops[{index, place, status, deliveredAt, completedSeq}], truck{truckId, plate, key, vehicleClass, vehicleClassKey}, partnerCode, sealCode, isMultiDelivery, incidentCount, photoCount}` plus, for staff, `deliveredVia`, `needsAdminReview`, `reviewStatus` and, with `accounting:view_income` (never a dispatcher), `price` (`{estimateThb, unpricedReason, manualOverride, jobCategoryAtPricing, isMultiDelivery, computedAt}`, the stored snapshot, null before pricing: a subset of `billing.TripBillingOf`, the one billing projection of a trip that `GET /v1/trips` and the trip detail carry whole as `billing`, read in the page's transaction so RLS applies R61, F59). The truck is the trip's snapshot, else its task's, never the driver's live truck (ADR 0005 §6); the place of a value is the hub stored at write (the `nameToCode` resolution of the ETL and the mirror, main spec §13.4), else its SOC key (stored, or SOCE/SOCN/SOCW and their spellings), else the trimmed raw text as its own bucket, else none (ADR 0006; labels as `lib/hubDisplay.ts`). Places are resolved at load, not on read (ADR 0006 §8 resolved in the browser on every read; ADR 0029's data model stores the hub): a trip loaded before its hub or alias existed stays a `raw:` bucket until it is re-resolved, which before cut-over is the `hub_unresolved` finding and `etl quarantine resolve --action=retry`; after cut-over nothing re-resolves (the owner may ask for a re-resolve step or a read-time fallback later), and in return a renamed hub no longer orphans its old trips. Values are trimmed with JavaScript's `trim` set (tab, line breaks, NBSP, the Unicode spaces, BOM; `lib/placeFilter.ts`, `lib/truckPlate.ts`) on both sides, in SQL (`btrim(x, trim_chars)`) and in Go, so a stored `'Bangna\t'` is the option `raw:Bangna`, its row key and its filter; the text inside is kept verbatim, and a `raw:`, `plate:` or `partner` filter value takes back any option the facets list (control characters included; only invalid UTF-8 and NUL, which no stored text holds, are `422`). The first page (no cursor, unless `facets=false`) carries `meta.facets = {total, statusCounts, incident, origins[], destinations[], plates[], vehicleClasses[], partners[]}`, counted over the whole range (and `customerId`), every option `{value, label, count}` plus `isUnresolved` (places) or `isOrphan` (plates): built from the trips, so every option matches a trip, the unresolved places, the orphan plates and the none buckets have their own options, and no trip is unreachable by every option (ADR 0005 §4, ADR 0006 §2-4).
- `GET /v1/incidents`: newest first on `created_at`, no cap; each report with `tripNo`, `driverName` and `mapPhoto`, `situation1Photo`, `situation2Photo` (`{fileId, key, url}`); staff rows add `tenantSource`, `reportedByUserId`, `legacyTripRef`, `updatedAt`. A scope principal reads the incidents of the trips it sees (`scope_incidents`): a customer whose parties have no trip reads none.

### B.2.13 Standby (staff)

| Method | Path | Capability / auth | Listener | Phase | Replaces | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| GET | `/v1/standby` | `operations:view_driver_monitor` \| `dispatch:view_operations` | internal | P2 | listener `standby-records/page.tsx:166-171`; `billing.ts:828-835,1466-1483`; `income:401-407` | `?from&to&customerId&driverId&cursor`, sort `ended_at DESC`; removes the 300-row cap (date filter was applied to the newest 300) |
| POST | `/v1/standby/backfill` | `operations:create_standby` | internal | P2 | `standby-backfill-dialog.tsx:301-357` | Driver body + `driverId`; `backfilled_by_admin=true`; same tx effects as the driver submit; outbox `standby.completed` |
| PATCH | `/v1/standby/{id}/customer` | `accounting:edit_rate_card` | internal | P3 | `assignStandbyCustomerAndPrice` (`billing.ts:1613-1632`) | `{customerId}` -> `customer_resolved_from='manual'`, then compute; repricing an already-priced record additionally requires `accounting:recompute_force`; outbox `standby.customer_assigned` |
| POST | `/v1/standby/{id}/billing/compute` | authenticated; `forceRecompute` requires `accounting:recompute_force` | internal | P3 | `computeStandbyBillingSnapshot` (`fn:standbyBilling.ts:228`; caller `billing.ts:1629`) | `{ok, skipped?, blocked?, invoiceNumber?, billingEstimateThb?, error?}`; closes the ungated force (`fn:standbyBilling.ts:238-261`); `ended_at` axis; unpriced reasons `no_customer`, `no_rate`, `no_ended_at` stored in `billing_unpriced_reason` (R62); customer resolution keeps ignoring `task.billingCustomerId` for parity (R19) |
| POST | `/v1/standby/{id}/notify-line` | `operations:edit_trip_details` | internal | P5 | `standby-records/page.tsx:143` | `{force?}` -> `202 {jobId, status}` (as the task row, T49) |
| POST | `/v1/standby/{id}/evidence/revoke` | `operations:edit_trip_details` | internal | P5 | — | `204`; sets `standby_records.evidence_token_revoked_at`; a new token is minted on the next forced LINE send (R47) (as the trip row, T49, legacy tokens included: `409 failed_precondition` before P5, `200 {revoked, legacyLinkOpen}` without a Firestore writer) |

**As built (T30, issue #50): `GET /v1/standby`.** `?sort=created|ended&from&to&driverId&customerId&limit&cursor`: the default lists every record newest first on `created_at` (a record without `ended_at`, unpriced `no_ended_at`, stays listed; refines "sort `ended_at` DESC"), `sort=ended` the completed records by `ended_at`, latest first; `from` / `to` bound the sort column; `customerId` matches the record's customer or billed party (staff; a scope principal `422 not_available`). Each record carries `driverName` and its photos (`[{type, overlayText, fileId, key, url}]`); staff rows add `tenantSource`, `customerPartyId`, `customerResolved`, `customerResolvedFrom`, `backfilledByAdmin`, `migratedFromTripNo`, `updatedAt`, and the `billing` block (`{estimateThb, partyId, rateSource, rateEntryId, effectiveFromDate, unpricedReason, computedAt, billingTenantId}`) only for the billing carrier's staff (`operations.Project`, Appendix C §C.3.4) holding `accounting:view_income`. A dispatcher or a customer-scope principal reads `scope_standby` and `scope_standby_photos`.

**As built (T31, issue #51): the operations writes.** Thirteen internal routes behind `auth.RequireAuth` and their capability (`operations:manage_tasks` for the task routes, `operations:edit_trip_details` for the trip routes and `POST /v1/incidents`, `operations:create_standby` for the backfill); an API key never writes them; a platform admin writes as the tenant it names in `X-Act-On-Tenant` (without it `403 permission_denied` with `details.reason=act_on_tenant_required` where the route guard lets it through, i.e. `PATCH /v1/tasks/{id}`). Bodies are JSON objects read field by field: an unknown field is `422 <field> not_supported`, a PATCH changes only the fields it names and `null` clears an optional one; no query parameter (`400`). The answers are the rows as the matching read route answers them (`GET /v1/tasks/{id}`, `GET /v1/trips/{id}`, a `GET /v1/standby` or `GET /v1/incidents` item; created rows `201`, else `200`), without the task-type read check of the boards (the writer holds the write key).

- **Which store.** `PG_OWNED_DOMAINS` decides (main spec §12.1). With `operations` in it (the staging VM, every environment from P7b) the route writes PostgreSQL in the caller's request transaction (`db.WithPrincipal`: RLS decides every row, a row the caller cannot update is `404`) and appends its events there (below). Without it the route writes the Firestore documents first, in the legacy shape of main spec §13.8, through `internal/writeback` (one `documents:commit` per request: all of its documents or none), never PostgreSQL next to them; it then waits up to 3 s for `cache:mirror_ack:{collection}:{docId}` at the commit time (the mirror's listener, T29) and answers like the PostgreSQL path, else `202 {"data": {"pending": true, "id", "collection", "docId", "updateTime", "document"}}` (the fields written; `pending: true` added to the import's and the bulk cancel's own answers). A Firestore-owned domain without `WRITEBACK_FIRESTORE_PROJECT_ID` answers `503 unavailable` with `details.reason=write_back_unconfigured`; a failed Firestore write `503` `write_back`; a Firestore precondition (the document a create would write exists, the one an update names is gone, the one a read-modify-write read changed; Firestore answers the missing document of an `exists` precondition with `404 NOT_FOUND` "No document to update", which `gcp.Firestore.Commit` reports as the precondition, while any other 404, a missing database or project, stays a `503`) `409 failed_precondition` `document_changed`; a master row a document must name by its Firestore id (driver, truck, party, task, trip) without one yet `409 failed_precondition` `not_projected` with `details.field`. Evidence photos uploaded to Go's storage cannot travel through the write-back yet (the mirror links Firebase Storage URLs only, and the trip and standby loaders replace a document's whole photo set, so a photo entry without one would be dropped): `photos` / `photoKeys` are `422 write_back_unsupported` there (main spec §13.8; the follow-up is on issue #51). A user-valued field of a write-back document (`reportedByUid`, `renamedBy`) carries the caller's Firebase uid (`users.legacy_auth_uid`), else, for a login born in Go, its `users.id`, which the mirror also resolves (`etl docCtx.userID`).
- **`POST /v1/tasks`** `{taskType (first_mile|line_haul, FIRST_MILE|LINE_HAUL accepted), date (a Bangkok day yyyy-MM-dd: its Bangkok midnight; or an RFC 3339 instant), time? (HH:MM), actualPickupAt?, sourceHub, sourceHubId?, destination, destinationHubId?, billingPartyId (required, ADR 0027), sourceLinkedPartyId?, destinationLinkedPartyId?, jobCategory? (PRIMARY|SUPPLEMENTARY, default PRIMARY on create), truckId?, truckType?, driverId?, helperDriverId?, isMultiDelivery?, deliveryStops?[{destination, destinationHubId?, destinationLinkedPartyId?, sourceId?, isCustom?}]}`. Places resolve to their hub as the load resolves them (SOCE/SOCN/SOCW keys first, then the hub codes the text carries; main spec §13.4), and a hub's linked party fills the source or destination link the body leaves out (the legacy forms' hub-derived link). The truck's plate and vehicle class are snapshotted from the truck master (an unknown class stays `NULL` with the raw type, R15; a `truckType` other than the truck's class is `422 truckType not_truck_class`); the driver, helper, truck and parties must be rows the caller reads (`422 <field> not_found`), the helper is never the driver (`422 helperDriverId same_as_driver`), an inactive driver is `409 failed_precondition driver_inactive`; the links keep the R13 mover's tenant rules (Appendix C §C.1.7, §C.9.2 #12; readable is wider than writable, a dispatcher reads other carriers' drivers and trucks through its parties' scope): the helper and the truck belong to the task's tenant or to one linked to it by `contractor_tenant_id` (R60) in the writer's own write reach (its tenant and contractor reach, never the dispatcher scope), else `422 <field> not_in_tenant` (the driver decides the tenant on create); stops: two or more, unique after trimming and upper-casing (`422 deliveryStops too_few`, `409 duplicate_destination`). PostgreSQL path: `operations.Stamp` (the driver's tenant `driver`, else the caller's active tenant `form`: a dispatcher's driverless call-out lands in its organisation's tenant, R13), the number from `next_task_seq()` inside the insert transaction (`FM|LH-ddMMyyyy-NNN`, R10; a day's counter at the int ceiling is `409 failed_precondition task_counter_exhausted`, never `500`), an assigned task at the end of its driver's queue under `LockDriverQueue`; status `assigned` with a driver, else `pending`; outbox `task.created` and, with a driver, `task.assigned` (the one visible push). Write-back path: `Stamp` checks the caller may write the tenant the row lands in (the mirror stamps the row), the number is allocated in a transaction of its own (a failed Firestore write leaves a gap, never a duplicate), the document is created under a minted uuidv7 that the mirror keeps as the row's id (R25); the queue position (`max(run_order) + 1` under the driver's queue lock) is also held in `cache:run_order:{driverId}` for 15 minutes and every later write-back position passes it (`cache.Cache.ReserveRunOrder`; a position the body sets raises it), because the task reaches PostgreSQL only when the mirror loads it and two assignments of one driver within the mirror's lag would otherwise share a position (a Redis failure keeps `max + 1`, logged). A driverless task created through the write-back is stamped by the mirror, which has no writer tenant for it: it lands in the quarantine tenant (D6) like any legacy driverless task, so a dispatcher's call-out needs the PostgreSQL path or a driver.
- **`PATCH /v1/tasks/{id}`**: a `tenantId` other than the task's (or a task the caller does not read) is the T28 move above; otherwise the ordinary update of the create's fields plus `status` (`pending` | `assigned` only: the lifecycle moves through check-in, delivery and cancel; `422 status not_settable`) and `runOrder` (with a driver, under its queue lock). Status and run order change only with the driver or when the body sets them (fixes `fn:tasks.ts:120-137`): a new driver of a pending, assigned or cancelled task makes it `assigned` (a cancelled one reopens, its cancellation cleared) at the end of the new driver's queue, no driver makes an assigned task `pending` and takes it out of the queue; the driver of a checked-in, in-transit or completed task does not change (`409 task_in_progress`), neither do the planned stops of a task past `assigned`. A dispatcher's update of another carrier's task changes 0 rows (`404`, Appendix C §C.9.2 #12). The driver, helper and truck rules apply to new or changed values only: a body that sends the task's current driver or helper back (a form posting its full state) is not refused because that driver has been deactivated since; a new driver must belong to the task's tenant (`422 driverId not_in_tenant`: another tenant's driver joins the task only through the T28 move with `tenantId`), a new helper or truck to that tenant or a linked one in the writer's reach (as on create). A class-only `truckType` of a task that keeps a truck of known class must be that truck's class (`422 truckType not_truck_class`, the truck's class wins as on create; `truckId: null` with a class clears the truck; a truck of unknown class takes one). Locks read in the update's transaction (on the write-back path before the Firestore commit), nothing written when one refuses: a moved Bangkok plan day of a task whose trip is delivered (or billing-stamped) and billed to a party on the plan axis (ADR 0027) reads the period lock of the trip's billing instant (its billing date, else the task's plan instant) and the new plan instant (`409 billing_period_locked` with `blockedInvoiceNumber`, main spec §6.10, as `PATCH /v1/trips/{id}` `deliveredAt`); the helper's payroll lock (the legacy dialog's check, server-enforced): a change of the helper moves the day from the old helper's pay round to the new one's, and a plan-day move of a task with a helper moves it between that helper's rounds (Bangkok period `YYYY-MM`, round R1 = days 1-15, R2 = 16 to the end, the round `hr` counts helper days in, by `plan_at`); an approved or paid payroll run of an affected helper there is `409 payroll_locked` with `details {field: helperDriverId, driverId, status, period, round}` (read through `task_helper_payroll_locks()`, SECURITY DEFINER, migration `0020`, so an operator without HR keys and a carrier admin whose old helper is the own fleet's are refused alike; draft and pending-approval runs are no lock). Events (the mirror's rules, T29): a driver set on a task without one `task.assigned`, a driver changed or removed `task.reassigned` (`driverId`, `previousDriverId`), anything else `task.updated` (`changed[]`); a moved Bangkok plan day adds `task.plan_date_changed`; a request that changes nothing writes nothing.
- **`POST /v1/tasks/{id}/cancel`** `{reason?}`: `cancelled` with `cancelled_at` and the reason, `task.cancelled` (the visible cancel push); a cancelled task is answered unchanged without an event, a completed one is `409 task_closed`. **`POST /v1/tasks/{id}/stops`** `{destination, destinationHubId?, destinationLinkedPartyId?, sourceId?, isCustom?}` (`addDeliveryStop`'s staff use): stop 1 bootstrapped from the task's destination, a destination the task has is `409 duplicate_destination`, the task becomes multi-drop; answer `{taskId, stops[]}`, event `task.updated`; the write-back appends to the document's own `deliveryStops` read from Firestore under its update time (retried three times on a concurrent change).
- **`POST /v1/tasks/import`** `{taskType, rows[{date, time?, sourceHub, destination, billingPartyId, jobCategory? (the cell text), truckType?, plate?, taskNo?, note?}], dryRun?, notify?}`: at most 1000 rows, 400 on the write-back path (`operations.WriteBackBatch`: the documents go in one Firestore commit, all of the import or none, so a failed commit leaves nothing to import twice; above it `422 rows too_many` with `details.fields[0].params.max`, before anything is numbered); each valid row is created as `POST /v1/tasks` creates (numbers sequenced server-side per row, no `count()+1`), each in a savepoint, so one row's refusal never undoes another; `jobCategory` goes through `compute.JobCategoryFromCell` (blank = PRIMARY, a misspelled cell such as `เสิรม` rejects the row); `plate` is matched to the fleet (upper case without JavaScript's `\s` and dashes on both sides, `normalizePlate`: NBSP, the Zs spaces, BOM and vertical tab included, `operations.plateStrip`; unknown `422 plate not_in_fleet`, two trucks `ambiguous`, blank allowed); `taskNo` (the sheet's shipment id) is kept verbatim, and a canonical padded one (`FM|LH-ddMMyyyy-NNN`) moves the global counter of its type and day past it, so it must name the import's type and its row's plan day (`422 taskNo mismatch`) with a sequence of at most 999 (`422 taskNo out_of_range`): an unrelated carrier cannot push a day's counter to the int ceiling and stop every tenant's creations of that day (a number kept by `CreateTask` past 99999, a legacy or mirrored one, moves no counter either); imported tasks are pending, so `notify: true` is `422 not_supported`. Answer `{created: [{row, id, taskNo}], errors: [{row, code, fields[]}], dryRun}` (a dry run lists the valid rows without ids).
- **`PATCH /v1/trips/{id}`** `{sealCode?, partnerCode?, ocrData? (object), deliveredAt?, needsAdminReview?, reviewStatus? (pending_review | null), reviewReason?, origin?, destination?, photos?[{type, key, lat?, lng?, address?, geocodedAt?}]}`: photos committed (purpose `trip_photo`) and merged by type; a `deliveredAt` change of a delivered trip with a billing party reads the period lock of the old and the new instant in the transaction (`409 billing_period_locked` with `blockedInvoiceNumber`, R17) and adds `trip.delivered_at_changed` (no realtime topic) to `trip.updated`. As built (T34, issue #54; the T31 handoff): `stops?[{destination, status? (pending|delivered|failed), deliveredAt?, lat?, lng?}]` is the whole new stop progress, its position the index (at most 50; each destination required, upper-cased and unique, `422 stops.destination required|duplicate`, `stops.status invalid`, with `params.index`): in the trip's `FOR UPDATE` transaction `trip_delivery_stops` is upserted by `stop_index` (a row keeps its id, so `trip_photos.stop_id` links survive; its `source_id` only while the destination does), the indexes past the new length are deleted, a stop keeps its own status and, while not pending, its delivery time and position unless the body names them (a pending stop has none; a newly delivered one gets the next `completed_seq`), and `is_multi_delivery` becomes `len > 1`. An edited `origin` re-derives `job_type` from the resolved place (a SOC key or a hub of station type SOC `line_haul`, another hub `first_mile`, an unresolved place leaves it; no free `jobType` field). The trip's task (when the caller can write it) mirrors the edit in the same transaction, exempt from the "not past `assigned`" rule (an admin correction): its route (`source_hub_*` with the hub's billing link, `destination_*`), its type (the number keeps its prefix), and its planned stops rewritten as stop 1..n, each with the fields of its old stop at the same destination (billing link, rates, sequence, `added_at`, `source_id`, `is_custom`), else the hub's billing link; `task.updated` carries the task's `changed[]` (`sourceHub`, `sourceLinkedPartyId` when the hub's billing link moves the task to another party, so the realtime projection sends that party's dispatchers a copy, `destination`, `taskType`, `deliveryStops`, `isMultiDelivery`), `trip.updated` adds `stops`, `isMultiDelivery`, `jobType`. The mirror is a task edit (Appendix C row 23): when it would change the task (in the caller's write reach), the caller needs `operations:manage_tasks` besides `operations:edit_trip_details`, else `403 permission_denied` with `details.missingCapability=["operations:manage_tasks"]` and nothing is written (on both paths), as `PATCH /v1/tasks/{id}` refuses the same change; a trip-only edit (seal, partner, delivery time, photos) needs `operations:edit_trip_details` only. A stop's completion order counts for a delivered stop only (the loader numbers every array position, so a pending or failed row's `completed_seq` is ignored and the same list sent again is no change). On the write-back path (operations Firestore-owned) one commit writes the trip document (`deliveryStopsProgress` with each stop's own `photos` and keys kept, in completion order as Appendix A reads the array (Q16: the loader's `completed_seq` is the array position): every entry the document holds keeps its position, an entry the loader cannot read (no usable index) stays unchanged, a removed stop leaves, added stops follow in plan order and the stops this edit delivers come last; the list is compared with the document's in plan order, so the same list is no write; `isMultiDelivery`, `totalDeliveryStops`, `jobType`) and the task document (`deliveryStops` with each stop's billing link kept, else the hub party's `destinationLinkedCustomer*`, `isMultiDelivery`, `taskType` literal, `sourceHub` with `sourceHubLinkedCustomer*`, `destination`), a read-modify-write under the update times read (a document changed in between is read again, twice, then `409 document_changed`); a party without a Firestore document is `409 not_projected`. A route or stop correction does not reprice: the web calls `POST /v1/trips/{id}/billing/compute` after it on a delivered trip, as the legacy dialog did. `POST /v1/trips/{id}/job-category` is T38's.
- **`POST /v1/trips/{id}/deliver`** `{deliveredAt?, lat?, lng?, photos?[]}` (the admin close of a stuck trip): an in-transit or incident trip becomes delivered with `deliveredVia=admin_web` (`deliveredAt`: the body's, else the trip's own, else now), its open task `completed` and the driver's active truck cleared when it still names that task, in one transaction (write-back: one batch over the trip, the task and the driver's `activeTruck`); outbox `trip.delivered` (billing.compute, notify.line) and the task's `task.updated`. Another status is `409 trip_not_open`. **`POST /v1/trips/{id}/cancel`**: the trip and its open task cancelled, `trip.cancelled` and `task.cancelled`; a cancelled trip is answered unchanged, a delivered one `409 trip_delivered`. **`POST /v1/trips/bulk-cancel`** `{ids[]}` (at most 200): the in-transit trips among them with their open tasks; answer `{cancelled[], failed[{id, reason: not_found|not_in_transit|not_projected, status?}]}`.
- **`POST /v1/trips/{id}/rename`** `{newTripNo}`: `operations:edit_trip_details` held as `tenant_admin` (a platform admin through `X-Act-On-Tenant`; others `403 tenant_admin_only`), as `renameTripRecord` was admin-only. The caller's RLS must let it update the trip (`404`), then `iam.TripRenamer` renames it in one system transaction (`security_events` is written in a system transaction only): `operations.RenameTrip` (delivered trips only `409 failed_precondition not_delivered`, `409 duplicate_trip_id`, `422 tripNo <reason>`), `trip_record_renamed` (warning, actor the caller, tenant the trip's) and `trip.updated` with `previousTripNo`. While Firestore owns the trips the rename stays `renameTripRecord`'s batch until T58: the document copied to the new number with `spxTripId`, `renamedFromTripId`, `renamedAt`, `renamedBy` (created only when no document holds the number), its `incidentReport` documents re-pointed, the old document deleted under the update time it was read with, all in one commit, the audit row after it; the mirror applies the batch as the same in-place rename (T29).
- **`POST /v1/standby/backfill`** `{driverId, taskId?, tripId?, startLocation, endLocation, startedAt, endedAt, note?, jobCategory?, truckId?, photos?[{type: customer_worksheet|site_photo, key, overlayText?}]}`: a completed record with `backfilled_by_admin`, the duration in minutes from the two instants (`422 endedAt before_start`), stamped `task` → `trip` → `driver`; a trip of another task than the one named is `422 tripId task_mismatch`; a driver other than the job's own (its task's or trip's) must belong to the record's tenant and a truck other than the job's to that tenant or a linked one in the writer's reach (`422 <field> not_in_tenant`, the link rules of `POST /v1/tasks`); in the same transaction its task becomes `completed` and its trip `standby`, as the legacy batch did; outbox `standby.completed` (billing.compute, notify.line), `task.updated`, `trip.updated`. An inactive driver is accepted (past work).
- **`POST /v1/incidents`** `{tripId?, driverId?, delayCause?, description, lat?, lng?, truckId?, photoKeys?{map, situation1, situation2}}`: an admin report (`reported_by_kind=admin`, `reported_by_user_id` the caller) on a trip or a driver (one of them required: `422 tripId required`; a trip names its driver when the body names none), stamped `trip` → `driver`, the truck's plate snapshotted, the photos committed with the report as owner (a driver other than the trip's must belong to the report's tenant, a truck other than the trip's to it or a linked one in the writer's reach: `422 <field> not_in_tenant`); outbox `incident.created`.
- **Events.** PostgreSQL path: the routing keys above with the realtime topics of §B.4.2 (`tenant:{tid}:tasks` or `:trips`, `dispatch:{partyId}:*` per party of the row, its task and its trip, `driver:{driverId}` of the driver, the helper and a previous driver for task events) and `payload.source=api`. Write-back path: only the writer's notify events, after the Firestore write (`task.assigned`, `task.reassigned`, `task.cancelled`, `task.updated`, `task.plan_date_changed`, `trip.delivered`, `standby.completed`), with the header `origin=writeback` (`outbox.OriginWriteBack`), `payload.source=writeback`, `payload.legacyDocId` (the document the write committed) and `payload.writeUpdateTime` (that commit's update time), and no realtime topic; the mirror's event of the same change (`origin=etl.sync`) carries the topics and feeds billing. `notify.fcm` plans a push from the task row, so it holds a write-back event (transient failures, the retry ladder) until `cache:mirror_ack:tasks:{legacyDocId}` reaches `writeUpdateTime`: read before the mirror applied the write, the row still shows the previous driver or status and the push would be lost, since the mirror's event pushes nothing. An acknowledgement lives 10 minutes, so a write older than that without one (expired, or lost to a Redis failure of the mirror) is taken as applied and planned from the row. So an assignment, a reassignment and a cancel push exactly once on either path, also when the write-back answered `202`.

### B.2.14 Billing: rate tables, fuel, rows, statements, documents, backfills

Money rule for every write in this table and in §B.2.12-§B.2.13: rate tables, fuel adjustments, service fees, standby rates, hub code maps and period locks are read from PostgreSQL inside the pricing transaction (`SELECT ... FOR SHARE` on the affected `billing_statements` rows); Redis copies serve the rate-card UI only (R17, R53). Rate tables, `trip_billing_snapshots`, `billing_counters` and `billing_statements` carry the **billing carrier's** tenant (the rate-card owner, the own fleet today), not the tenant that ran the trip (R61), so `accounting:*` capabilities apply in that tenant; the standby billing columns stay inline on `standby_records` and the service projection hides them from the executing tenant.

Output under `documents/reports/{jobId}/`; jspdf leaves the web bundle at P3 exit (R69) |

| POST | `/v1/billing/statements` | `accounting:billing_document` | internal | P3 | `saveBillingStatement` (`billingStatement.ts:128-168`; counter incremented before `addDoc`, `:84-94` -> numbering gaps) | `{customerId, year, month, types[], categories[], note?, showActualPickup?}`; one tx: rows from stored snapshots (never repriced), invoice number from the SECURITY DEFINER allocator `next_invoice_seq()` (row lock on `billing_counters (billing_party_id, period_year, period_month)`, R66) -> `{CODE}-{YYYYMM}-{SEQ:03}` with no gap on failure, `withholding_tax_rate` snapshotted from the issuing company (R18), `billing_statement_lines` persisted; outbox `statement.created` -> `documents.render` (live from P3, R69: invoice summary PDF, detail XLSX and bundle ZIP into `statement_documents`) |
| GET | `/v1/billing/statements/{id}` | `accounting:billing_result` | internal | P3 | `billing-result/page.tsx` | Statement + `documents[]` `{kind, status, url?}` from `statement_documents` (R14) |
| GET | `/v1/billing/statements/{id}/lines` | `accounting:billing_result` | internal | P3 | `billing-result/page.tsx` | `billing_statement_lines` (row id, row type, amount) |
| POST | `/v1/billing/statements/{id}/status` | `accounting:manage_statements` | internal | P3 | `billingStatement.ts:256-267` | `{status: sent\|paid\|cancelled, paymentDate?, paymentMethod?}`; draft -> sent -> paid, any -> cancelled; `sent`/`paid` lock the period (port of `fn:core/billingPeriodLock.ts`); `paid` renders the receipt; outbox `statement.status_changed` |
| DELETE | `/v1/billing/statements/{id}` | `accounting:manage_statements` | internal | P3 | `billingStatement.ts:276-278` | Draft only, otherwise `409 statement_not_draft` |
| GET | `/v1/billing/statements/{id}/documents/{kind}` | `accounting:billing_result` | internal | P3 | client downloads `billing-document/page.tsx:475`, `billing-result/page.tsx:280` (jspdf / xlsx-js-style / jszip in the browser, `web:lib/billingDocument.ts:12-16`) | `kind = invoice_summary_pdf\|invoice_detail_xlsx\|bundle_zip\|receipt_pdf` -> `302` to a presigned GET of the rendered object; `409 failed_precondition` (`details.status` = `pending\|rendering\|failed`) while not ready. jspdf and xlsx-js-style leave the web bundle at P3 exit (R69) |
| POST | `/v1/billing/statements/{id}/documents/regenerate` | `accounting:manage_statements` | internal | P3 | client regeneration `billing-result/page.tsx:269-304` | optional body `{showActualPickup?}` (as built T39); `202 {jobId}`; renders invoice PDF, detail XLSX and ZIP from `billing_statement_lines` (a re-download equals the original); `paid` statements also re-render the receipt |
| POST | `/v1/jobs/billing.backfill-trips` | `accounting:edit_rate_card`; `forceRecompute` requires `accounting:recompute_force` | internal | P3 | `backfillTripBillingSnapshots` (`fn:tripBillingOnDelivered.ts:990`; callers `rate-card/page.tsx:728`, `income/page.tsx:835`) | `{fromDateStr, toDateStr, maxScan?, maxWrite?, forceRecompute?, customerId?}` -> `202`; result `{scanned, eligible, written, skipped, failed, blocked, blockedInvoices[], failures[], capped}`; guard `lock:job:billing.backfill:{customerId\|all}`; queue `billing.backfill` |
