**As built (T37, issue #57).** `internal/billing` serves the rate-table, statement and fuel routes above and `POST /v1/jobs/bangchak.snapshot` on the internal listener behind `auth.RequireAuth`; requests run in `db.WithPrincipal` (RLS G-tenant on the billing tables, the reads naming the tenant reach; the fuel tables are RLS-exempt). Writes need an effective tenant (`authz.RequireTenant`): it is the billing carrier stamped on every rate row, statement and counter (`tenant_source 'form'`, R61). Every rate-table and statement write (import, manual add, voids, fuel rounds, fees, standby rates, statement create / status / delete) needs that tenant to be the **own fleet** (its staff, or a platform admin acting on it with `X-Act-On-Tenant`); any other tenant is `403 permission_denied` with `details.reason = own_fleet_only`, whatever capability it holds (T37 review: a carrier `tenant_admin` holds `accounting:edit_rate_card`, billing parties are global and the fee slot is per party, so an unrelated carrier could otherwise squat or feed the own fleet's rate card). Carrier self-billing is the open owner decision of main spec §19 Q13. `customerId` in a request names a billing party: its `billing_parties` id, or the customer or carrier it bills (an id that names no party: `422 customerId not_found` on a write, an empty list on a read); responses carry `billingPartyId` (the party), `customerId` (the customer or carrier the party bills: `customers.id`, or the carrier's tenant id, the ids `GET /v1/customers` and the web's `['rateCard', table, customerId]` keys use) and `tenantId`. Refinements of the rows above:

- Lists: the four rate-table lists take `?customerId`; with it they are served from `cache:ratecard:{partyId}` (all four tables of the party, every tenant, voided rows included, loaded READ ONLY in `db.WithSystem` and filtered to the caller's tenant reach), without it from PostgreSQL. Rate entries page by keyset `(effective_from_at, import_id by bytes, id)` DESC (`?limit&cursor`); `?includeVoided=true` adds voided rate entries, fuel rounds (added here) and standby rates; fees and standby rates are returned whole (one party holds a handful).
- Rate entries: `effectiveFromDateStr` is a strict `yyyy-MM-dd` (no roll-over); the vehicle class goes through `vehicleclass.Of` (blank or outside the task enum `422`, R15), `jobCategory` through `JobCategoryFromCell` (an unknown cell `422`, never PRIMARY); an import is all or nothing (`rows[i].field`, at most 5000 rows); the manual row answers `201` with the row.
- Fuel adjustments: `pct` at most 4 decimals (`1 + pct/100`); band `fuelBandBaselineFuelFloor` / `fuelBandThbPerBaht` absent or 0 take 41 / 10 (the page's `|| 41`, `|| 10`) and are stored as used; each row carries `fuelBand {lowerThb, upperThb}` of its reference price (`FuelBandRange`).
- Service fees: `feeType` is any of the six `customer_service_fees` types (`extra_stop`, `waiting_time`, `special_handling`, `service_charge`, `standby`, `custom`), `unit` `per_trip|per_stop`; the party of a fee and of a standby rate does not change on `PATCH` (`422 customerId immutable`). Standby rates take `effectiveFromDateStr` like the other tables; a voided rate is `409 failed_precondition` on `PATCH` and on a second void.
- Voids answer `200` with the row; a second void of a rate entry or fuel round is `409 announcement_immutable` (`details.reason = already_voided`).
- Statements: the create body adds `issuerCompanyId?` (default the billing tenant's owner company, else its only active company); `types` / `categories` absent = all; nothing billable `409 failed_precondition` (`no_billable_rows`), a counter of another billing carrier `409 failed_precondition` (`counter_owned_by_other_carrier`); `201` with the statement. The status body is `{status}`: `billing_statements` has no payment date or method columns, so `paymentDate` / `paymentMethod` are not accepted into storage. `GET .../{id}` and the `201` list `documents[]` (`[]` before the render; absent from list rows) without URLs (the download route is T39); list and detail carry `generatedByName` (the creator's `users.display_name`, else email; null when the creator is not visible), so the billing-result page needs no per-creator `users` reads (Appendix E); `GET .../{id}/lines` adds `rowId` and `tripNo`. The invoice set is read under the caller's RLS and first counted without it: rows the caller cannot see (a trip or standby record this carrier priced but run by a tenant outside its reach, or quarantined) are `409 failed_precondition` (`rows_outside_reach`, `details.hiddenRows`), never left off silently; standby records count by their stored carrier `standby_records.billing_tenant_id` (`0015`; without one, the carrier of the rate entry the record names, else the own fleet). The withholding is computed from the rate the insert stored (R18). A status change takes the period's exclusive advisory lock before the row's `FOR UPDATE`. Pricing paths (T38) read the period lock with `billing.PeriodLock` (shared advisory lock of each period, then every statement of the months `FOR SHARE`, main spec §6.11) and the rate tables of one carrier with `billing.LoadPricingTables(party, carrier)` (carrier `billing.BillingCarrier`, the own fleet).
- Fuel: `GET /v1/fuel/daily` `?from&limit&cursor` (newest first), `GET /v1/fuel/monthly` `?limit` (default 36) `&cursor`; `GET /v1/fuel/retail` without `locale` is `th`, another value `422`; the feed off or failing is `503 unavailable` (`details.reason` `bangchak_disabled` / `bangchak_unavailable`), never cached. `POST /v1/jobs/bangchak.snapshot` takes `lock:job:bangchak.snapshot:all` and is steward-only (`authz.RequireSteward`): a carrier's `tenant_admin` holds `accounting:edit_rate_card`, but a run before 05:00 Bangkok on a price-change day would freeze the insert-only daily row every tenant's band rounds reference.

**As built (T39, issue #59).** The read aggregates and the documents of the rows above, on the internal listener behind `auth.RequireAuth`, in `db.WithPrincipal`:

- `GET /v1/billing/rows`, `GET /v1/billing/rows/missing-billing-date`, `GET /v1/billing/standby-diagnostics` describe the own fleet's invoicing: another tenant is `403 permission_denied` (`own_fleet_only`), whatever capability it holds, as the statements they preview. List parameters (`types`, `categories`) are repeated keys, the `[]` form or one comma-separated value; absent = all, empty or unknown `422`; an unknown parameter `400`. `customerId` names a party as in T37 (`all` for every party on the delivered axis, rows and diagnostics only); an id that names no party answers an empty period. The rows answer `{customerId, billingPartyId, year, month, basis, periodFrom, periodTo, types, categories, rows[], unpriced[], invoice, preview, typeCounts, categoryCounts, issues, filterOptions{plates, vehicleClasses, reviewMonths}, outsideReach, documentable}` (main spec §6.14 as built: the row fields, `inInvoiceSet` = the statement's lines for the toggles, `inPreview` narrowed by `?plate` (`id:{truckId}`, `plate:{plate}`, `__none__`), `?vehicleClass` (folded class, `__none__`) and `?reviewMonth` (`yyyy-MM`, `__none__`), which never change the invoice set; unpriced rows with `reason` + `reasonSource` `stored|dry_run`, the dry run adding `not_computed`, `task_missing`, `multi_insufficient_stops`). Diagnostics answer `{unpriced[], missingEndedAt[], missingEndedAtTruncated}` (the flat list of the row above is refined to this object); missing-billing-date a list of `{tripId, tripNo, deliveredAt, priced}`. Every parameter that changes the rows answer is in its W6 key (Appendix E §E.4): `plate`, `vehicleClass` and `reviewMonth` change `inPreview` and the `preview` totals, `types` and `categories` the invoice set, `inInvoiceSet` and `documentable`.
- `GET /v1/billing/shopee-report` (`?customerId&year&month&half=full|first|second`, default `full`) answers `{customerId, billingPartyId, year, month, half, periodFrom, periodTo, trips[], tripCount, driverCount, photoCount, missingPhotoCount}` under the caller's RLS; each trip carries `signedRunsheetPhotos[{photoType, fileId}]` and no price. `GET /v1/billing/shopee-report.pdf` needs a staff tenant reach (`403` otherwise: the worker renders the trips of the reach the request names); the job's parameters are `{partyId, year, month, half, reach, allTenants, tenantIds}` (`reach` a digest of the tenant reach, so a render is reused only for callers that see the same trips) and its result `{fileId, key, fileName, rowsVersion, tripCount, photoCount, missingPhotoCount}`; the route answers `302` (`Cache-Control: no-store`) to a signed download of the newest succeeded render whose `rowsVersion` equals the version of what the render would print now, else starts the job (`lock:job:documents.shopee-report:{partyId}:{yyyymm}:{half}:{reach}`: a running render answers its own `jobId`, only to callers of the same reach, who can read the job and receive its `job.updated`; another reach starts its own render) and answers `202 {jobId}`. `rowsVersion` is a SHA-256 digest of everything the PDF prints but its issue date: the customer and owner-company names of the header and, per trip in print order, its number, delivery time, driver and name, origin and destination labels (`hubs.name_en` / `name_th` through the snapshot's lookup codes or the task's raw names), vehicle class and plate (the task's before the trip's), parcel count and the files of its signed run-sheets. A correction of the trip, its task, driver, snapshot, a hub label or a run-sheet (one removed included) makes the render stale, and a change committed after a render read its rows does too; a write that changes nothing printed keeps it current. The worker and the route read that content the same way, in one READ ONLY system transaction that names the reach of the job's parameters (Appendix C §C.3.2), so the caller's RLS cannot make the two digests differ. `jobs.TypeCapabilities` lists `documents.shopee-report` (`accounting:shopee_report`) and `documents.regenerate` (`accounting:manage_statements`), so the tenant's staff holding them read the job.
- `GET /v1/billing/statements/{id}/documents/{kind}` (`invoice_summary_pdf|invoice_detail_xlsx|bundle_zip|receipt_pdf`; another value `404`) reads the statement under RLS (`404` when hidden) and answers `302` to a signed GET of the stored file (documents cap it at 15 min) with `Content-Disposition: attachment` and the names of main spec §9.9 as built; a document not rendered yet, rendering or failed is `409 failed_precondition` with `details.status` (`pending|rendering|failed`, `details.error` when failed), a receipt of an unpaid statement `409 failed_precondition` (`statement_not_paid`). `GET /v1/billing/statements/{id}` lists each document with `url` = this route once it is `ready` (the web calls it under the BFF prefix `/api/go`; it is not a presigned URL). `POST /v1/billing/statements/{id}/documents/regenerate` is own-fleet only like every statement write and answers `202 {jobId}` (`409 already_exists` with `jobId` while one runs).
- The detail sheet's optional `วันรับงานจริง` column (`tasks.actual_pickup_at`, ADR 0028; the Billing Document page's toggle) is stored with the statement, `billing_statements.show_actual_pickup` (`0016`): `POST /v1/billing/statements` takes `showActualPickup` (boolean, absent = `false`), the statement (detail and list) carries it, and `POST .../documents/regenerate` takes the optional body `{showActualPickup}` (boolean; absent = the stored choice; another type `400`), renders the detail sheet and bundle with that choice and stores it in the transaction that succeeds the job, so the column always names what the stored sheet shows.
- A render that cannot succeed (an error drawing a document) marks its kinds `failed` with the cause; on `statement.created` / `statement.status_changed` the message is acked as permanent (no retry ladder, no dead letter) and a regenerate recovers it; a storage or database failure returns to the retry ladder.
- `documents.rendered` carries `{billingPartyId, statementId, status, kinds[]}`.
- `billing.TripBillingOf` / `billing.IncomeBillingVisible` are the billing projection `GET /v1/trips` (§B.2.12, issue #50) attaches for `accounting:view_income`.

**As built (T38, issue #58).** Compute-on-delivery and the billing writes of the rows above (main spec §6.13 as built):

- `POST /v1/trips/{id}/billing/compute` and `POST /v1/standby/{id}/billing/compute` are served below the operations groups' routers (`operations.Groups` mounts `billing.TripRoutes` / `billing.StandbyRoutes`): a caller that does not read the record under its RLS gets `404`; amounts, invoice numbers, the unpriced `reason` and its `error` message are answered only to the own fleet's staff holding `accounting:view_income` (as the billing blocks of `GET /v1/trips`, §B.2.12); any other caller, a carrier that ran the record included, gets `{ok, skipped?, billingDateMoved?}` (standby: `{ok, skipped?, blocked?}`); `forceRecompute` needs `accounting:recompute_force` and the own fleet (`403 own_fleet_only`). A forced trip reprice blocked by a sent or paid old or new period is `409 billing_period_locked` (`details.blockedInvoiceNumber`, `periodYear`, `periodMonth`), nothing written; a blocked standby reprice keeps the callable's `200 {ok: true, skipped: true, blocked: true, invoiceNumber}`. For those callers both answers add `reason` (a stable code: the R62 reason, `task_missing`, `multi_insufficient_stops`, `not_delivered`, `stale`, `billing_not_owned`).
- `POST /v1/trips/{id}/job-category` takes `{jobCategory}` in any case, needs the own fleet besides `accounting:edit_rate_card`, answers the compute shape; refusals: `422` with the unpriced reason as code (`no_rate`, `no_customer`, `no_vehicle_class`, `no_billing_date`), `409 failed_precondition` (`not_delivered`, `task_missing`, `multi_insufficient_stops`), `409 billing_period_locked`; the same target on task and trip is `{ok: true, skipped: true}`. The task's category is a class C field (§B.5.3 synchronous write-back): with a write-back port configured it is merged into `tasks/{legacy_doc_id}` (`{jobCategory, updatedAt}`) before the transaction commits, and a failed merge is `503 unavailable` (`details.reason write_back`) with nothing kept.
- `PUT /v1/trips/{id}/billing/manual` needs the own fleet besides `accounting:override_price`; `billingEstimateThb` > 0 with at most 2 decimals and `jobCategory` (`PRIMARY|SUPPLEMENTARY`, any case) are required (`422 invalid_argument`); answers the compute shape; `computed_by 'manual_edit'`; a manual amount on a multi-drop trip is stored as one amount (no breakdown).
- `PATCH /v1/standby/{id}/customer` needs the own fleet besides `accounting:edit_rate_card`; `customerId` is a billing party or the customer or carrier it bills (`422 customerId not_found`); answers the standby compute shape; a priced record needs `accounting:recompute_force` and is repriced, and a blocked reprice rolls the customer change back (`409 billing_period_locked`). With a write-back port configured the customer is merged into `standby_records/{legacy_doc_id}` (`{customerId, customerResolved, customerResolvedFrom: 'manual', updatedAt}`, the legacy page's write) before the commit (`503` on failure, nothing kept).
- `POST /v1/jobs/billing.backfill-trips|backfill-standby|impact-report` (each its own group next to `/v1/jobs`) need the own fleet; the lock is `lock:job:{type}:{partyId|all}` (per type, so the two backfills of one month run side by side; the impact report `all`); `maxScan` / `maxWrite` outside their bounds are `422 out_of_range` (never clamped); the impact report refuses `maxWrite`, `forceRecompute` and `customerId` (`422 not_allowed`). The job types join `jobs.TypeCapabilities` (backfills `accounting:edit_rate_card`, impact report `security:view_overview`).

### B.2.15 Expenses and maintenance (staff)

| Method | Path | Capability / auth | Listener | Phase | Replaces | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| GET | `/v1/expenses` | `accounting:view_fuel` (type `fuel`) \| `accounting:view_other` (type `other`) \| `accounting:audit_expense` | internal | P3 | `getVehicleExpensesByType` (`features/accounting/api/expenses.ts:90-101`, scans every type and filters in the browser; drivers x2, trucks x2); audit full reload on status change `audit/page.tsx:106-108` | **W8 server filter**: `?type=fuel\|other&status&category&driverId&truckId&from&to&cursor` (`category` as built, T40), keyset on `(date, id)`; `type` and `status` are both optional (no `type` = every type the principal may read; no `status` = all statuses), and `status` is both in the query key and the SQL, so the audit page no longer reloads everything on a status switch; plate comes from the frozen row snapshot; never dispatcher; infinite query `['expenses',{type,status,category,driverId,truckId,from,to}]` 60 s, virtualised table (W9) |
| POST | `/v1/expenses` | `accounting:edit_fuel` \| `accounting:edit_other` | internal | P3 | `createFuelExpense`/`createOtherExpense` (`expenses.ts:394-481`) | Admin-entered (`status=approved`, `created_by`) |
| PATCH | `/v1/expenses/{id}` | `accounting:edit_fuel` \| `accounting:edit_other` | internal | P3 | `expenses.ts:322-370` | `distanceKm`, `adminNote`, truck (sets `truck_id` and plate together), amounts |
| POST | `/v1/expenses/{id}/status` | `accounting:audit_expense` | internal | P3 | `expenses.ts:279-319`, `audit/page.tsx:175,203,229` | `{status, adminNote?}`; approving a fuel row with odometer runs the PM check in the same tx; outbox `expense.approved` (+ `maintenance.created` when due) |
| POST | `/v1/expenses/bulk-status` | `accounting:audit_expense` | internal | P3 | `audit/page.tsx:175,203,229` | `{ids[], status}` -> rows |
| POST | `/v1/expenses/toll-import` | `accounting:edit_other` | internal | P3 | `expenses.ts:222-260`, `TollExpenseImportDialog.tsx` | XLSX/JSON -> `{created, skipped}`; `toll_import_sequence` server-side |
| GET | `/v1/maintenance` | `fleet:manage_maintenance` | internal | P3 | `web:features/maintenance/api/maintenance.ts:72,253-257` | `?truckId&status&type&cursor` (`type` PM/CM as built, T40); status normalised `pm_booking, scheduled, in_progress, completed, cancelled`; never dispatcher |
| POST | `/v1/maintenance` | `fleet:manage_maintenance` | internal | P3 | `maintenance.ts:110-143` (non-atomic) | One tx: `maintenance_records` row + truck status maintenance + `active_maintenance_id`; outbox `maintenance.created` |
| PATCH | `/v1/maintenance/{id}` | `fleet:manage_maintenance` | internal | P3 | `maintenance.ts:160-241` | Validated status transition; completion writes last service date, mileage, next service mileage, clears `active_maintenance_id`, restores truck status, all in one tx; outbox `maintenance.updated` |
| POST | `/v1/maintenance/{id}/remind` | `fleet:manage_maintenance` | internal | P3 | `notifyMaintenanceReminder` (`fn:triggers.ts:362`; caller `features/maintenance/api/maintenance.ts:53`) | `{title?, body?}` -> `202`; outbox `maintenance.reminder_requested` -> FCM `maintenance_scheduled` |

**As built (T40, issue #60).** `internal/expenses` serves `/v1/expenses*` and `internal/maintenance` serves `/v1/maintenance*` and `POST /v1/trucks/{id}/pm-check` (mounted on the `/v1/trucks` group by `fleet.Groups`), every statement in `db.WithPrincipal` (RLS G-tenant: the caller's reach, own tenant plus the carriers working for it; the list and lookup queries name the same reach, `authz.Principal.TenantReach`; a dispatcher or customer scope holds none of the capabilities and RLS gives it no row). **List (W8):** `GET /v1/expenses?type=fuel|other&status&category&driverId&truckId&from&to&limit&cursor`; `type` and `status` are SQL filters (the legacy `PENDING|APPROVED|REJECTED` accepted), no `type` = every type the caller may read (`accounting:view_fuel` reads `fuel`, `accounting:view_other` reads `other`, `accounting:audit_expense` both; a named type the caller may not read is `403 permission_denied` with `details.missingCapability`), `from`/`to` are inclusive Bangkok days on `expense_date`; keyset `(expense_at, id)` newest first, no cap; the first page carries `meta.statusCounts {pending, approved, rejected}` over the other filters (the audit page's tabs). On a table where tolls outnumber fuel logs the fuel page reads `vehicle_expenses_type_date` with the type in its index condition (the integration test checks the plan of the query as served). **Payload** `{id, tenantId, type, category, status, date (the instant), expenseDate (Bangkok day), amountThb, driverId, driverName, truckId, licensePlate (the row's frozen snapshot), volumeLiters, pricePerLiter, odometerKm, stationTaxId, taxInvId, refillLat, refillLng, note, description, adminNote, distanceKm, tollImportSequence, tollLocation, tollLane, tollSourceType, receiptKey, odometerKey, createdBy, createdAt, updatedAt}`; photos are object keys read through `GET /v1/files?key=`. **Writes:** `POST` takes `{type, driverId? | truckId?, date (RFC 3339, a Bangkok wall time or a day), amountThb, status? (default approved), …, receiptKey?, odometerKey?}` with the type's edit capability; the tenant is the driver's (`tenant_source 'driver'`, else the truck's), the truck defaults to the driver's active truck, else its home truck, and its plate is snapshotted; without `adminNote` the legacy texts ("บันทึกโดย admin (ข้อมูลจาก petro app)" / "บันทึกโดย admin") are kept; an approved fuel entry with an odometer reading runs the PM check like an approval (a refinement: the legacy page never checked admin entries) and locks its truck before inserting the row (the insert's foreign-key check takes `FOR KEY SHARE` on the truck, so taking the PM check's `FOR UPDATE` afterwards deadlocked two entries of one truck). Fuel-only fields (`volumeLiters`, `pricePerLiter`, `stationTaxId`, `taxInvId`, `odometerKey`) on an other row and other-only fields (`category`, the toll fields) on a fuel row are `422 not_available` (a null or blank one counts as absent). `PATCH` takes the same fields minus `type`, `driverId`, `status` (`422 not_editable`; `status` has its own route), `truckId` sets the truck and its plate snapshot together (null clears both), `receiptKey`/`odometerKey` null unlinks; outbox `expense.updated` lists `changed[]` (added to the expenses family, §B.4.2). **D5:** a fuel row's `(driver_id, tax_inv_id)` is checked under a transaction-scoped advisory lock and by the `vehicle_expenses_fuel_taxinv` index of `0010` (where it is applied): `409 duplicate_tax_invoice` with `details.field = taxInvId` on `POST`, `PATCH` and four concurrent entries alike. **Audit:** `POST /v1/expenses/{id}/status {status, adminNote?}` and `POST /v1/expenses/bulk-status {ids[] (≤ 500, every id in reach or `422 ids not_found` and nothing written), status, adminNote?}` lock the rows in id order; a blank note keeps the stored one; a request that changes nothing writes nothing; a row that becomes approved emits `expense.approved` (`expense.status_changed` otherwise, payload `previousStatus`) and, when it is a fuel row with a truck and an odometer reading, runs the PM check in the same transaction after every row is written, trucks in id order; the answer is the row(s) with `pmCheck: {created, maintenanceId, outcome} | null`. **PM check** (port of `checkMaintenanceAlert` and `core/maintenance`): the truck row is locked (`FOR UPDATE`), so concurrent approvals and bulk approvals of one truck create exactly one PM booking; outcomes `created`, `no_next_service_mileage`, `active_maintenance` (a terminal active record is cleared with the alert mark first), `already_alerted` (`last_alert_mileage`, 0 when unset, equals the reading), `open_maintenance` (a `pm_booking` or `scheduled` record exists), `within_threshold` (the truck's `current_mileage` moves), `truck_not_found` (outside the reach; the route answers `404`); a booking is `{type PM, serviceType "เช็คระยะตามรอบ", startDate = today on the Bangkok calendar (the legacy code used the UTC date), status pm_booking, the readings, the legacy automatic note with en-US digit grouping}`, becomes the truck's `active_maintenance_id`, sets `last_alert_mileage` and `current_mileage`, and emits `maintenance.created`; `POST /v1/trucks/{id}/pm-check {mileage (0-10,000,000)}` runs it alone. **Toll import:** `POST /v1/expenses/toll-import` takes `multipart/form-data` (`file`: XLSX first sheet or CSV, form fields `driverId`, `truckId`, `dryRun`) or JSON `{driverId, truckId, dryRun?, rows: [{sequence?, location?, lane?, sourceType?, date, amountThb}]}`, at most 5000 rows, read within the bounds of `internal/platform/xlsx` (`429 resource_exhausted` while two reads run); the column detection, the toll-type test (ผ่านทาง, ทางด่วน, expressway, easy pass, mflow, toll) and the date rules (Excel serial with its time, d/m/y with an optional time, a two-digit or Buddhist-era year, ISO) are the dialog's, on the Bangkok wall clock; every toll row becomes a pending other expense (category `toll`) of the driver (tenant) and truck (plate snapshot) with its sequence (the row's position when the file has none), place, lane, source type and the legacy description; answer `{created, skipped, skippedRows: [{row, reason}], dryRun}` with reasons `not_toll`, `invalid_date`, `invalid_amount`, `duplicate_in_file` and `duplicate` (the truck already holds a toll row of that instant, amount, place and lane: a re-imported history adds nothing; the imports of one truck run under a transaction-scoped advisory lock, so two overlapping imports of one history add it once); one `expense.created` per import (payload `count`, `source: toll_import`); a sheet without date, type and amount columns is `422 file missing_column`. **Maintenance:** `GET /v1/maintenance?truckId&status&type&limit&cursor` (keyset `(created_at, id)`, first-page `meta.statusCounts`), payload `{id, tenantId, truckId, licensePlate, truckBrand, truckModel, truckOwnership, truckStatus, type, serviceType, startDate, endDate, status, appointmentTime, pickupAppointmentAt, costLaborThb, costPartsThb, totalCostThb, invoiceAmountThb, displayCostThb (maintenanceDisplayCost), provider, providerLat, providerLng, paymentMethod, currentMileage, nextServiceMileage, driverSubmitted, checkInAt, checkOutAt, notes, files: {images, receipts, invoices} (keys), createdBy, updatedBy, createdAt, updatedAt}`; statuses are answered and stored lower_snake and taken in the legacy literals too (`PM Booking`, `Scheduled`, `In-Progress`); `POST` (status default `in_progress`, the form's) and `PATCH` lock the truck, then the record, and in one transaction write the record, its files (`files: {images|receipts|invoices: [keys]}`, purpose `maintenance_file`, kinds replaced), the truck (an open record becomes the truck's active maintenance unless another open one is; `in_progress` puts the truck in status `maintenance` with its `status_history` entry; `completed` and `cancelled` return an in-shop truck to `active`, clear the active maintenance and the alert mark, and `completed` writes `last_service_date` = `endDate`, `current_mileage` and, for PM, `next_service_mileage`; a completed record's corrected end date or mileages are re-applied, as the legacy form did), outbox `maintenance.created|updated` (topics `tenant:{tid}:fleet` and `driver:{id}` of the truck's drivers: active assignments and the active-truck driver) and `truck.updated`; transitions: `pm_booking` and `scheduled` to any other status, `in_progress` to `completed` or `cancelled`, `completed` and `cancelled` final (`409 failed_precondition` `details.reason invalid_transition` with `from`, `to`); `truckId` and the driver's fields (`driverSubmitted`, `checkInAt`, `checkOutAt`) are not editable. A PM booking moved to `scheduled` or `in_progress` emits `maintenance.reminder_requested` (the legacy page's `notifyMaintenanceReminder` call). `POST /v1/maintenance/{id}/remind {title? (≤ 100), body? (≤ 500)}` answers `202 {eventId}` for an open record whose truck has a driver (`409 failed_precondition` `not_open` / `no_driver` otherwise). **Pushes:** `notify.fcm` sends `maintenance_scheduled` (legacy data keys and channel) to the drivers of the record's truck, read server-side, for `maintenance.reminder_requested` and for `maintenance.created` of a record created `scheduled` (the status in the event; the legacy page pushed on no creation, and a PM booking or a record created `in_progress`, the form's default, has no appointment to announce); without staff text the title is "นัดเช็คระยะ" and the body the legacy "รถ {plate} วันที่ … เวลา … ที่ … — กรุณาเข้าอู่ตามนัด"; a record closed by the time the message is read pushes nothing. **Write-back (class C, until P7b):** not wired in T40: both services write PostgreSQL only (right on a PostgreSQL-owned domain and on the staging VM). On a Firestore-owned domain the class C write follows main spec §11.10 and §13.7 (Firestore written first in the legacy shape, PostgreSQL filled by the FS->PG mirror, the handler waits up to 3 s for `cache:mirror_ack`, else `202`) and lands with T31 on the port `internal/writeback` (`Writer.Merge` with update masks, a Firestore REST adapter over `internal/etl/gcp`, an in-memory stand-in); T40 ships the encoders it uses, `expenses.LegacyWrite` and `maintenance.LegacyWrite` (upper-case expense status, the maintenance literals with `in_progress` as the web's `in_progress` until `check_in_at` is set and `In-Progress` after, `driverId` = the Firebase uid, `truckId` = the trucks document id, `refillLocation "lat,lng"`; a created row writes every staff field, a change only the Firestore fields of the JSON fields it changed, so the fields the 3.x app writes on the same document stay the app's). **Contention:** a deadlock or serialization failure the database reports (`40P01`, `40001`) rolls the request back and answers `503 unavailable` with `details.reason = contention` (retryable), never `500`.

### B.2.16 Chats and broadcasts (staff)

| Method | Path | Capability / auth | Listener | Phase | Replaces | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| GET | `/v1/chats` | `chat:view` | internal | P5 | `ConversationsPanel.tsx:127-163`, `ChatStatusWidget.tsx:19-36`, `with-driver/page.tsx:32-34`, `chat/layout.tsx:69` | `?filter=queue\|mine\|all&driverId&status&cursor`, sort `last_message_at DESC`; `queue` = unassigned and not closed; per-chat `unreadForMe`; `['chats','queued']`, `['chats','mine']` 15 s |
| POST | `/v1/chats` | `chat:send` | internal | P5 | `with-driver/page.tsx:48-62` | `{driverId}` -> the driver's open chat or a new one (partial unique on non-closed chats, R14) |
| GET | `/v1/chats/{id}` | `chat:view` | internal | P5 | `room/page.tsx:73-74` | Chat + read state |
| GET | `/v1/chats/{id}/messages` | `chat:view` | internal | P5 | unbounded history listener `room/page.tsx:89-91` | `?before&limit=50` keyset DESC on `(created_at, id)`; `['chat',id,'messages']` infinite, SSE appends via `setQueryData` |
| POST | `/v1/chats/{id}/messages` | `chat:send` | internal | P5 | `room/page.tsx:165-194` + `notifyChatMessageCreated` (`fn:chat.ts:77`, no auth; caller `room/page.tsx:172`) | `{text?, imageKey?, clientMessageId}` (`client_message_id` unique per chat, R63); one tx: insert, `last_message_*`, auto-assign + `in_progress` on the first admin reply, sender read state; outbox `chat.message_created` -> FCM to the driver (to the assigned admin on driver messages only when `NOTIFY_ADMIN_ON_DRIVER_MESSAGE` is on) |
| POST | `/v1/chats/{id}/read` | `chat:view` | internal | P5 | `lastReadByAdmin.{uid}` written inside the snapshot callback (`room/page.tsx:108-113`, `:140`) | `{lastReadAt?}` -> `204`; `chat_read_state (chat_id, user_id)` |
| POST | `/v1/chats/{id}/assign` | `chat:send` | internal | P5 | `room/page.tsx:177-238` | `{adminUserId?}` (default self) |
| POST | `/v1/chats/{id}/close` | `chat:send` | internal | P5 | `room/page.tsx:177-238` | — |
| POST | `/v1/chats/{id}/reopen` | `chat:send` | internal | P5 | `room/page.tsx:177-238` | — |
| PATCH | `/v1/chats/{id}` | `chat:send` | internal | P5 | `room/page.tsx:177-238` | `{priority: normal\|urgent}` |
| GET | `/v1/broadcasts` | `broadcasts:view` | internal | P5 | `BroadcastComposer.tsx:139-144`, `BroadcastHistoryModal.tsx:97-102` | `?limit&cursor`; includes read counts from `broadcast_reads`; voided ones left out (as built T48, below) |
| POST | `/v1/broadcasts` | `broadcasts:send` | internal | P5 | `sendBroadcast` (`fn:chat.ts:135`; caller `BroadcastComposer.tsx:205`) | `{recipientUserIds[] \| recipientGroup, title, messageText}`; recipients persisted (`broadcast_recipients`); a platform-wide recipient group (drivers of every tenant) is accepted only from stewards (R60); outbox `broadcast.created` -> batched FCM; Firestore projection for installed APKs until P7b. Gate corrected (R5) |
| DELETE | `/v1/broadcasts/{id}` | `broadcasts:send` | internal | P5 | `BroadcastComposer.tsx:178`, `BroadcastHistoryModal.tsx:80` | Soft delete `broadcasts.voided_at` (R14) -> `204` |

**As built (T47, issue #67; the chat rows above and the six `/v1/mobile/chat*` rows of §B.2.21).** `internal/chats` serves both on one service, every statement in `db.WithPrincipal` (RLS decides: staff the chats of their tenant reach, contractor reach included, a driver its own; another tenant's chat is `404`); the staff routes admit people only. **List:** `?filter=queue|mine|all&status&driverId&limit&cursor` (`queue` the default; `limit` 50, at most 500; a bad value `422 invalid_argument`, an unknown parameter `400`), newest message first with a chat that has none last, keyset `(last_message_at, id)`; items `{id, tenantId, legacyDocId, driverId, driverName, driverUserId, driverLegacyUid, status, priority, assignedAdminUserId, assignedAdminName, assignedAt, closedAt, lastMessagePreview, lastMessageAt, lastMessageByUserId, lastMessageType, createdAt, updatedAt, unreadForMe, lastReadAt}`: `unreadForMe` counts the other side's messages after the caller's read state, in SQL (driver messages for staff, staff messages for the driver), `lastReadAt` is the caller's. **Detail** (`GET /v1/chats/{id}`, `GET /v1/mobile/chat`) adds `reads[{userId, lastReadAt, isDriver}]` and `driverLastReadAt`. **Open** (`POST /v1/chats {driverId}`, `POST /v1/mobile/chat`): the driver's chat that is not closed (`200`) or a new one in the driver's tenant (`201`, `chat.created`; an unknown driver `422` `driverId` `not_found`); one transaction-scoped advisory lock per driver serialises opens and reopens, so one open chat per driver holds before `0010` builds `chats_one_open_per_driver` too; on the write-back path that lock also covers the lookup of a chat document the mirror has not applied yet (a non-closed chat with the driver's `driverId`) and the creation of a new one, so retries and concurrent opens before the mirror catches up answer the same document (`202`, `docId`). **Messages:** `?before&limit` (`before` = the `nextCursor` of the previous page), newest first; items `{id, chatId, legacyDocId, senderUserId, senderRole, senderName, text, imageFileId, imageUrl, messageType, clientMessageId, createdAt}` (`imageUrl` signed for `S3_PRESIGN_GET_TTL`; `senderName` the staff's display name, null for a driver reader). **Send:** `clientMessageId` (a uuid) required, `text` (at most 4000 characters, trimmed) and/or `imageKey` (purpose `chat_image`, `entityId` the chat; committed in the transaction); `201` the message; the stored message of a replayed `clientMessageId` is `200` and nothing is written, the same id with another message (another sender, text or image) `409 idempotency_conflict`. The first staff reply assigns the chat to its sender (`in_progress`); a staff reply to a closed chat reopens it (`in_progress`), `409 already_exists` (`details.reason open_chat_exists`, `details.chatId`) while the driver has another open chat; a driver's send goes to its open chat, created when it has none (on the write-back path into the chat document found or created as for an open, at once, `202`); when staff closes that chat between the lookup and the send, the send runs once more and lands in a new chat (`201`), never `409 chat_closed`; a driver's `clientMessageId` is looked up across all its chats first, so a replay that arrives after staff closed the chat answers the stored message (`200`) and opens no chat. The `Idempotency-Key` of the `✱` row is the offline outbox's (T56, with every `✱` route); `clientMessageId` is the durable replay key either way (R63). **Read:** `{lastReadAt?}` (RFC 3339; absent = now) moves the reader's state forward only and never past now, `204` (`POST /v1/mobile/chat/read` without an open chat: `204`, nothing). **Assign:** `adminUserId` absent = the caller, `null` = back to the queue (`open`), a user = an active staff member who reaches the chat's tenant (its own, or the contractor it works for, R60) with `chat:view` and `chat:send` in that membership's role set, overrides included (else `422` `adminUserId` `not_found`: the `user` role, another tenant's staff, a driver); a closed chat `409 failed_precondition` (`details.reason chat_closed`). **Close / reopen / priority:** close keeps the assignment; reopen gives `in_progress` when assigned, else `open` (`409 already_exists` as above); a write that changes nothing emits nothing. Every write answers the chat as `GET /v1/chats/{id}` does. **Unread count:** `{count, messages}` (the driver's open chats with unread staff messages, and those messages; `count` is the legacy badge). **Write-back:** while any domain is not in `PG_OWNED_DOMAINS` (chats are class C until P7b: `comms` alone, P5, keeps the write-back; gate corrected by the T48 review) the writes take the class C write-back of main spec §13.8 as built T47 (one Firestore commit per request; `202` with the document written when the mirror did not acknowledge within 3 s; a replay of a `clientMessageId` the mirror has not loaded is answered from the stored message document, `409 idempotency_conflict` for another message under it; the chat document gone `409 failed_precondition` `document_changed`; a Firestore failure, or a push event not appended after the commit, `503 unavailable` `write_back`, retried with the same `clientMessageId`; an image is `422 write_back_unsupported`); the Firestore writer is the api's write-back project of T31 (`WRITEBACK_FIRESTORE_PROJECT_ID`, the operations write-back's); without it they answer `503 unavailable` (`details.reason write_back_unconfigured`) and PostgreSQL is never written in Firestore's place. Events: §B.4.3; the push: `notify.fcm` (§B.5.3, main spec §7.6 as built T47).

**As built (T48, issue #68; the three broadcast rows above and the three `/v1/mobile/broadcasts*` rows of §B.2.21).** `internal/broadcasts`, every statement in `db.WithPrincipal` (RLS decides, migration `0022` adds the driver's addressed broadcasts to `broadcasts.p_read`); the staff routes admit people only. **List:** `?limit&cursor` (50 by default, at most 200; the legacy history's silent cap of 50 is gone), newest first, keyset `(sent_at, id)`; the caller's reach (its tenant and the carriers working for it) and the platform-wide broadcasts; voided broadcasts are left out; each item `{id, tenantId, legacyDocId, createdByUserId, createdByName, title, messageText, recipientGroup, recipientCount, readCount, sentAt, voidedAt}`, `readCount` = `COUNT(broadcast_reads)`. **Send:** exactly one of `recipientGroup` (`all_driver`, `own_fleet`, `subcontractor`, or the platform-wide `all_tenants`) and `recipientUserIds[]` (stored as group `selected`), plus `title` and `messageText` (both required, trimmed; 300 and 5000 characters at most); the legacy `recipientDriverIds` list is not accepted. The server resolves the audience under the caller's RLS: the linked logins of the drivers that are not inactive, whose login is `active` or `reset_required` and whose tenant is active; `own_fleet` = the caller's tenant's drivers whose `employment_type` is not `subcontractor`, `subcontractor` = its `subcontractor` drivers and the drivers of the carriers working for it, `all_driver` = both (the legacy composer's split, now in the caller's reach); `all_tenants` = the drivers of every tenant (`broadcast_platform_audience()`, `0022`), stewards only (`403 permission_denied` otherwise, R60) and stored with `tenant_id` NULL; `recipientUserIds` must each be such a driver's login of the reach (`422`, `recipientUserIds[i]` `not_found`). While any domain is not PostgreSQL-owned (before P7b: installed 3.x APKs may be the recipients' app) a `recipientUserIds` send is `422` (`recipientUserIds` `unsupported_until_p7b`, before the database; T48 review): the 3.x app lists every document of the projected collection and the Firestore rules let any signed-in user read it, so the message could neither stay private there nor reach its recipients' 3.x list; the group sends stay open, and every 3.x driver lists a projected group send until P7b, as every legacy broadcast was. An empty audience is `422` (`no_recipients`); a send needs a staff member acting in a tenant (`403 tenant_required`; a platform admin acts through `X-Act-On-Tenant`). One transaction writes the broadcast, its recipients and `broadcast.created`; `created_by_name` is the sender's display name, else its email; `201` with the broadcast. **Void:** `DELETE /v1/broadcasts/{id}` sets `voided_at`, `voided_by` and appends `broadcast.voided` (no realtime topic; `firestore.shadow` deletes the Firestore document); a broadcast the caller cannot read is `404`, a platform-wide one needs a steward (`403`), one voided already answers `204` again without an event. **Driver:** `GET /v1/mobile/broadcasts` (`?limit&cursor`, 50 by default, at most 100) lists the broadcasts addressed to the driver (a `broadcast_recipients` row) and those without persisted recipients it may read (the loaded legacy and seeded rows, addressed by their group), never a voided one, each `{id, ref, createdByName, title, messageText, sentAt, read, readAt}` (`ref` the Firestore document id, else the uuid); `GET /v1/mobile/broadcasts/latest` the newest of them or `null`; both take a driver token (`mobile:broadcasts`). `POST /v1/mobile/broadcasts/{id}/read` is the `markBroadcastRead` shim target (`auth.RequireShim`, `shim_requests_total{callable="markBroadcastRead"}`; APK 4.x Go tokens join with T55): `{id}` is the uuid or the Firestore document id; `200 {broadcastId, alreadyRead}`, the `broadcast_reads` primary key making it idempotent per user (concurrent first reads store one row, the others answer `alreadyRead: true`); a broadcast that is not one of the driver's (unknown, voided, another tenant's not addressed to it, or its own tenant's addressed to other drivers: only the broadcasts `GET /v1/mobile/broadcasts` lists, so `readCount` counts recipients) is `404` with `details.reason` `broadcast_not_found`, which the shim answers as the legacy `not-found` "Broadcast not found"; a first receipt appends `broadcast.read` (no realtime topic) in its transaction, which projects the readers. **Which store:** broadcasts are class B, written in PostgreSQL whatever `PG_OWNED_DOMAINS` says (like the holidays); `firestore.shadow` projects them once `comms` is owned, a carrier's and a selected send skipped, `readCount` / `readDriverAuthIds` merged on `broadcast.read` (main spec §13.8 as built T48, with the P5 order and the rollback); `comms` in `PG_OWNED_DOMAINS` moves no chat write (as built T47 above).

As built (T44): `202 {jobId}` for the caller's effective tenant; the lock is `lock:job:payroll.run:{tenantId}:{period}:{round}` (runs are per tenant) plus an advisory transaction lock of the same triple in the consumer; a run in progress is `409 already_exists` (`details.jobId`); no config in the tenant is `409 failed_precondition` (`no_compensation_config`); the run covers the tenant's own drivers only, priced with the version in effect on the month's last Bangkok day (`effective_from_at <` the month's exclusive end), and schedules only the tenant's own open penalties (drivers of carriers working for the tenant are not in its runs: main spec §6.18 #28, owner question §19.2 Q21); main spec §6.15 "As built" |

As built (T44): from `draft` or `pending_approval`; `409 failed_precondition` `stale_run` (`details.penaltyIds`) when an applied penalty is no longer open or below its application (regenerate first) |
| POST | `/v1/payroll/{id}/status` | `hr:manage_payroll` | internal | P4 | `payroll/page.tsx:155-164` | `{status: pending_approval\|paid\|cancelled, paymentDate?, paymentMethod?}` with validated transitions; outbox `payroll.status_changed`. As built (T44): also `draft` (re-open from `pending_approval` or `cancelled`) and `remarks?`; `approved` is `422` (`use_approve`); a refused move is `409 failed_precondition` (`transition_not_allowed`, `details.status`, `details.to`); approved and paid runs are never cancelled |
| GET | `/v1/penalties` | `hr:manage_payroll` | internal | P4 | `penalties.ts:39-74` | `?driverId&status[]`. As built (T44): `status` repeated or comma-separated, `?limit&cursor`, keyset `(incurred_at, id)` newest first (the legacy list stopped at 200); each row carries `installmentThb`, the next R2 deduction at most |
| POST | `/v1/penalties` | `hr:manage_payroll` | internal | P4 | `penalties.ts:39-74` | penaltySchema (integer THB). As built (T44): `{driverId, typeCode, typeName?, totalThb >= 1, installmentsTotal? 1-1000, reason?, evidenceKey?, incurredAt}` -> `201`; the driver (in the caller's reach) stamps the tenant; `typeName` defaults to the type's `nameEn` in the version in effect at `incurredAt` |
| POST | `/v1/penalties/{id}/cancel` | `hr:manage_payroll` | internal | P4 | `penalties.ts:76-81` | As built (T44): open penalties only (`pending`, `partially_deducted`); else `409 failed_precondition` (`penalty_closed`) |
| GET | `/v1/payroll/config` | `hr:manage_payroll` | internal | P4 | `config.ts:37-76` | `?at=` effective version; `['compConfig','active'\|'all']` 10 min. As built (T44): without `at` `{data: [versions]}` newest effective first (the caller's tenant); with `at` (RFC 3339 or a Bangkok day) `{data: version \| null}`; a version is `{id, tenantId, effectiveFrom, effectiveFromDate, weekdayRateThb, holidayRateThb, payStandby, helperDayRateThb, fuelIncentiveTiers[{minKmPerLitre, amountThb}], fuelMinRefuelsPerMonth, tripVolumeTiers[{minTrips, amountThb}], sso{ratePercent, baseExistingThb, baseNewThb, existingHiredBeforeYear, maxAgeInclusive, probationMonths}, penaltyTypes[{code, nameEn, nameTh, defaultAmountThb}], createdBy, createdAt}` |
| POST | `/v1/payroll/config` | `hr:manage_payroll` | internal | P4 | `config.ts:37-76` | Appends a new effective-dated version (append-only). As built (T44): the body of the version (absent fields take the `compensationConfigSchema.ts` defaults) -> `201`; whole-baht rates and tiers, unique penalty codes; `effectiveFrom` as a Bangkok day is stored as Bangkok midnight, so a version dated the 1st first prices that month's runs (`?at=` is inclusive, a run uses the version before the month's exclusive end) |

**As built (T45, issue #65).** `internal/hr` serves the leave and holiday routes above on the internal listener behind `auth.RequireAuth`, every statement in `db.WithPrincipal`.

- **`GET /v1/leave-requests`**: also `?limit` (default 50, max 500); `status` repeated or comma-separated, legacy upper case accepted; the caller's reach (own tenant + the carriers working for it, `authz.Principal.TenantReach`; quarantined rows hidden); keyset `(created_at, id)` newest first, no cap; the first page carries `meta.statusCounts` (every status, the driver filter applied). A request is `{id, tenantId, driverId, driverName, type: sick|business, status, startDate, endDate (Bangkok days), days, reason, approverUserId, decidedAt, rejectionReason, attachments[{key, url}], createdAt, updatedAt}`; `key` is always the file's object key and `url` a 15-minute presigned GET of the `leave_evidence` file (main spec §9.5), `null` for a file it cannot sign (missing at source after the ETL, not committed), as main spec §10 as built T25 item (9) lists such files (`{key, url: null}`).
- **Decisions**: approve and reject from `pending` only, cancel from `approved` only, else `409 failed_precondition` with `details.reason = transition_not_allowed`, `details.status` (the current one) and `details.to`; `reject` requires `{reason}` (at most 2000 characters), `approve` and `cancel` refuse one (`422`). An approval or a rejection records the staff user and the time (`approverUserId`, `decidedAt`, the legacy `approverId` / `approvedAt`); a cancel keeps them. Two paths (main spec §13.8 as built T45): while any domain is still Firestore-owned the 3.x APKs write leave requests in Firestore (class C), so the decision is a **write-back**: checked against the document's own status, written in the legacy shape under an update-time precondition (`409 failed_precondition` `document_changed` when the document changed or vanished meanwhile, `not_in_firestore` for a row that has no document), then the handler waits up to 3 s for `cache:mirror_ack` and answers `200` with the mirrored row, else `202 {pending: true, id, status, collection: "leave_requests", docId, updateTime, document}`; a Firestore failure is `503 unavailable` (`write_back`); the write goes through T31's `writeback.Writer` over the one Firestore client the api builds for its write-backs. Only when `WRITEBACK_FIRESTORE_PROJECT_ID` is unset and not every domain is PostgreSQL-owned does a decision answer `503 unavailable` (`write_back_unconfigured`), without writing anything. Once every domain is PostgreSQL-owned (`PG_OWNED_DOMAINS=all`: P7b, and a deployment without Firestore such as the staging VM) the decision is one PostgreSQL transaction (the request locked first, so two concurrent decisions leave one). The event is `leave.decided` (§B.4.3 as built).
- **`GET /v1/holidays`**: the public calendar (`tenant_id` NULL) and the caller's effective tenant's own holidays, every type and status, by day; a caller without a tenant reads the public calendar; `?year` (four digits, 2000-2100) narrows to the Bangkok year; strong `ETag` over the body, `If-None-Match` (weak or strong) -> `304`, `Cache-Control: private, no-cache`. A holiday is `{id, tenantId, date (yyyy-MM-dd), type: public|company|other, status: draft|published|archived, name, nameEn, nameTh, description, descriptionEn, descriptionTh, isRecurring, createdBy, createdAt, updatedAt}`.
- **Writes**: bodies name the fields of a holiday (`date` as `yyyy-MM-dd` or an RFC 3339 instant, its Bangkok day; `type` and `status` lower or the legacy upper case; the legacy `holidayNameEN` / `holidayNameTH` accepted for `nameEn` / `nameTh`; the read-only fields `id`, `tenantId`, `createdBy`, `createdAt`, `updatedAt` ignored; any other field `422 not_supported`); `name` falls back to `nameTh`, then `nameEn` (the loader's rule), and is required. `POST` defaults `type` to `public` and a new row's `status` to `draft`, upserts on `(tenant, day, type)` (`201` created, `200` updated with the fields named; absent fields keep the row's, `null` clears an optional text). `PATCH` is partial; `type` moves only between `company` and `other` (`422 type scope_change`: delete and create instead); another holiday's day and type is `409 already_exists`. A public row is a steward's only (R60): anyone else gets `403 permission_denied` before the database (RLS `p_write` refuses as well); `company` and `other` rows are the caller's effective tenant's, and the own fleet's staff reach their carriers' (contractor reach). `PUT /v1/holidays/generate` takes `{year, finalHolidays[] (<= 400), initialHolidays[] (<= 400)}`, every date inside the year (`422 out_of_range`), and in one transaction deletes each initial holiday the final list no longer holds where it exists, then writes every final holiday as `published` (created, or the existing holiday of its day and type updated with the fields it names); a final holiday repeating an earlier one's day and type is not written: `{saved, deleted, duplicates}` (the generator of `shared-docs/logic/thaiHolidays.ts` yields such a substitution day on a Songkran day). Every change appends `holidays.changed` (§B.4.3 as built), which `firestore.shadow` projects once `hr` is owned (main spec §13.8 as built T45).

### B.2.18 Mobile release and installations (staff side, internal only, R43)

| Method | Path | Capability / auth | Listener | Phase | Replaces | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| GET | `/v1/app-releases` | `security:manage_mobile_release` \| `security:view_mobile_clients` | internal | P6 (pulled forward 2026-10-11, T52S) | `mobile-release/page.tsx` reads of `settings/mobile_app` | `?flavor&limit&cursor`; rows of `mobile_app_releases` newest first (`released_at DESC, id DESC`): `{id, flavor, version, buildNumber, apkKey, apkUrl, apkSizeBytes, apkSha256, releaseNotes, releasedAt, releasedBy, storageBackend, latest}`; `meta.settings` is the gate document (the public fields plus `minAllowedVersionSetAt`, `minAllowedVersionSetBy`, `releasedBy`, `updatedAt`). `releasedBy` and `minAllowedVersionSetBy` only for `security:manage_mobile_release`. People only. The current floor and latest release are also read through `GET /v1/mobile/settings` (§B.2.21), which the internal listener also serves |
| PUT | `/v1/app-releases/floor` | `security:manage_mobile_release` | internal | P6 | `mobileAppSettings.ts:65-96` | User principals only (API keys rejected). `{minAllowedVersion}` (strict semver: canonical `x.y.z`, no leading zeros or build suffix; `null` removes the floor); refused while the flavor has no published APK URL (a floor without a download link would trap drivers, ADR 0007; `409 failed_precondition`, `details.reason = no_apk_url`) and above the latest release (`422`, `above_latest`); writes only the floor half of `settings['mobile_app']` (release publishing never writes it); `mobile_floor_changed` in tx (severity warning, `details {from, to, flavor, latestVersion, apkDownloadUrl, blocked7d}`); outbox `settings.changed` (cache delete, SSE, Firestore projection for installed APKs once T24 consumes it). `200` with the gate document and `meta {changed, blocked7d}`; the same floor again writes nothing. An API key is `403 permission_denied` (`api_key_scope`). As built in T52S (pulled forward 2026-10-11) |
| GET | `/v1/app-installations` | `security:view_mobile_clients` | internal | P6 (pulled forward 2026-10-11, T52S) | listener `mobile-clients/page.tsx:78-99`; `mobile-release/page.tsx:116` | `?tenantId&since&belowVersion&flavor&limit&cursor` (`since` RFC 3339 or `7d`/`24h`), sort `last_seen_at DESC` (keyset `(last_seen_at, driver_id, install_id)`); tenant context required (`X-Act-On-Tenant: *` for a platform admin); RLS reach: carrier `tenant_admin` sees its own tenant, own-fleet staff their carriers too; rows `{driverId, driverName, installId, tenantId, tenantName, platform, appVersion, buildNumber, flavor, firstSeenAt, lastSeenAt, versionStatus}` (`versionStatus` = `blocked\|outdated\|current\|ahead\|unknown` against the gate, the port of `lib/mobileVersion.ts`), `meta {flavor, minAllowedVersion, latestVersion}`; web polls 60 s while visible (W7) |
| GET | `/v1/app-installations/stats` | `security:view_mobile_clients` | internal | P6 (pulled forward 2026-10-11, T52S) | `fetchSecurityOverviewStats.ts:63-68` | `?since=7d&belowVersion=&tenantId=` -> `{since, total, byStatus {current, outdated, blocked, ahead, unknown}, byFlavor, byPlatform, belowVersion {version, count}\|null, flavor, minAllowedVersion, latestVersion}` (`belowVersion.count`: installations a floor of that version would lock out, unparseable versions never counted, `countBlockedByFloor`); tenant context and RLS as the list |
| POST | `/v1/app-releases/presign` | `apikey` (scope `release_publisher`, `security:manage_mobile_release`) \| `security:manage_mobile_release` | internal | P6 | `scripts/publish-mobile-release.mjs` (Storage upload) | `{flavor, version, buildNumber, sha256, sizeBytes, force?, switchFlavor?}` -> presigned PUT to the public bucket (`S3_PUBLIC_BUCKET`), immutable key `app_releases/{flavor}/logitrack-{flavor}-v{version}.apk`: `{key, url, method, headers, expiresAt, storageBackend, publicUrl}` (`Cache-Control: no-store`); `409 already_exists` (`upload_pending`) for an unpublished upload without `force`, and always for a published version (`published`; `force` never replaces one); `409 failed_precondition` (`flavor_mismatch`, `details.flavor`) for the other flavor than the deployment's gate flavor without `switchFlavor`; a flavor switch is refused while a floor is set (`409 failed_precondition` `floor_set`, `details {flavor, requested, minAllowedVersion}`: the floor would move onto the other fleet; a person removes it first with `PUT /v1/app-releases/floor {minAllowedVersion: null}`), and so is a release that would become the latest below the floor (`409 failed_precondition` `floor_above_version`); publishing never writes the floor (T52S review). The URL is signed for `S3_PRESIGN_ENDPOINT` (R74) with `Cache-Control: public, max-age=31536000, immutable` and `x-amz-meta-{sha256,build-number,flavor,version}` signed; on the local backend it is `PUT /v1/uploads/local/{key}` on the internal listener (up to 200 MiB there; `PUT /media/{key}` refuses an APK) (T52S). Called by the publish CLI `cmd/release` from the private network (on the VM, or a CI job over SSH, R43) at `GO_API_INTERNAL_URL` with the `release_publisher` key from `RELEASE_API_KEY` (R82) |
| POST | `/v1/app-releases` | `apikey` (scope `release_publisher`, `security:manage_mobile_release`) \| `security:manage_mobile_release` | internal | P6 | `scripts/publish-mobile-release.mjs` (Firestore write) | Called by `cmd/release` like the presign route. Server HEADs the object, verifies sha256 and size, commits its public `file_objects` row (`purpose='apk'`), inserts `mobile_app_releases`, updates the release half of `settings['mobile_app']` (download URL under `S3_PUBLIC_BASE_URL`); never writes `minAllowedVersion` (ADR 0007 two-writer rule; `PUT /v1/app-releases/floor` rejects API keys so a person always presses the button); outbox `settings.changed`. As built (T52S): body as the presign plus `releaseNotes?` (2000 characters); the sha256 is the local sidecar's or the S3 object read back; `422 invalid_argument` on `key` (`upload_missing`, `size_mismatch`, `content_type_mismatch`, `checksum_mismatch`) or on `sha256` / `sizeBytes` when the body differs from the upload; `released_by` = the user's email or `apikey:{name}`; the release half moves only for a version not below the gate's latest (else recorded, `meta.latest = false`) or for a flavor switch; the presign's refusals `flavor_mismatch`, `floor_set` and `floor_above_version` are checked again under the row lock (a flavor switch is refused while a floor is set; publishing never writes the floor); `mobile_release_published` in tx; `201 {data: release, meta {latest, replayed: false}}`, and `200` with `replayed: true` for a retry of the same build (checked first: a replay writes nothing, whatever the gate says now); `409 already_exists` (`published`) for another build of a published version. `cmd/release` rerun after a lost answer gets `published` from the presign and sends this route alone, so it reaches the replay without uploading again |

### B.2.19 Dashboard, badges, waitlist, partner interest

| Method | Path | Capability / auth | Listener | Phase | Replaces | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| GET | `/v1/dashboard/summary` | authenticated (each block gated by its capability) | internal | P6 | about 10.7k docs per visit: `DashboardStats.tsx:112-146`, delivered-trip ids `DashboardStats.tsx:175-178`, `ActivityChart.tsx:45-49`, `RecentUpdates.tsx`; incidentReport read twice, `vehicle_locations` x2 | **W8 aggregate**: `?from&to`; SQL aggregates incl. on-time % and histogram computed server-side; blocks: trips (`operations:view_driver_monitor`), trucks/compliance (`fleet:view_trucks`), incidents (`operations:view_incidents`), expenses (`accounting:view_fuel` \| `accounting:view_other`), chats (`chat:view`), activity series (`reporting:view_analytics`); `['dashboard','summary',{period}]` 60 s, polled 60 s while visible |
| GET | `/v1/badges` | authenticated (each counter gated by its capability) | internal | P6 | whole-collection waitlist listener `app-sidebar.tsx:65`; `ExpenseAuditWidget.tsx:17-24`; `ChatStatusWidget.tsx:19-36` | **W8 aggregate**: `{waitlist?, pendingExpenses?, unassignedChats?, urgentChats?}` gated by `waitlist:view`, `accounting:audit_expense`, `chat:view`; `['badges']` staleTime 30 s, polled 60 s while visible, plus SSE invalidation (Appendix E) |
| POST | `/v1/waitlist` | public | internal | P6 | `WaitlistModal.tsx:75`, `useWaitlist.ts:40` (anonymous Firestore write) | Posted by the marketing page to the unauthenticated BFF route handler `POST /api/forms/waitlist` (R44, R77), which forwards here; `rl:public_form_ip` 5/h/IP and `rl:public_form_email` 1/day/email (`RATE_LIMIT_PUBLIC_FORMS`), honeypot field; written under `WithSystem` (`waitlist` is RLS-exempt); outbox `waitlist.created` (badge SSE) |
| POST | `/v1/partner-interest` | public | internal | P6 | `join-network/actions.client.ts:20` | Through BFF `POST /api/forms/partner-interest` (R77); same limits; stored as `partner_interest.payload` |
| GET | `/v1/waitlist` | `waitlist:view` | internal | P6 | `waitlist/page.tsx:25` | `?cursor` |
| DELETE | `/v1/waitlist/{id}` | `waitlist:view` | internal | P6 | `waitlist/page.tsx:46` | `204` (same capability as today's page; no separate manage key exists in the catalog) |

**As built (T53, issue #73).** `internal/dashboard` serves the summary and the badges, `internal/forms` the forms and the waitlist; every read runs in `db.WithPrincipal`.

- **Summary** `?from&to`: Bangkok days, both included; default the 7 days ending today; at most 366 (`422 invalid_argument`: `invalid_date`, `after_to`, `range_too_long`). Blocks: `trips {delivered, parcels, withIncident, onTimePct, delayPct}` (delivered trips by `delivered_at`; a trip with an incident report is delayed; percentages `Math.round(x*1000)/10`, null without a trip), `activity {days[{date, delivered}], total, previousTotal, trendPct}` (zero-filled; the window of the same length before; the trend rule of `ActivityChart.tsx:130-135`), `incidents {inPeriod}`, `drivers {active, onDuty, inactive, total}` (`drivers:view`), `expenses {pendingFuel?, pendingOther?}`, `chats {unassigned, urgent}`, `compliance {taxDue, insuranceDue, serviceDateDue, serviceKmDue, items[≤50], itemsTotal}` (active trucks of the caller's tenant, every tenant in reach under `X-Act-On-Tenant: *`; due within 30 days or the next service at most `PMAlertThresholdKm` away; urgency order of `ComplianceSummary.tsx`), `recent[≤8] {type, id, at, plate?, place?, name?, text?}` (delivered trips: `operations:view_driver_monitor`; driver updates: `drivers:view`; incidents: `operations:view_incidents`; completed maintenance: `fleet:manage_maintenance`). A dispatcher or customer-scope principal gets `trips`, `activity`, `incidents` and `recent` from `scope_trips` / `scope_incidents` only (`internal/scope/repo/scope_dashboard.sql`, vetted by scope-views-only) and no other block. The role counts of the legacy users card are `GET /v1/stats/users-by-role` (memberships are platform rows).
- **Badges**: `{waitlist?, pendingExpenses?, unassignedChats?, urgentChats?}`, a counter present only for its key; `pendingExpenses` is every expense type in `pending`; the chat counters are open chats without an assignee (urgent among them); no query runs for a principal without a counter.
- **Forms**: internal listener only (the public listener answers `404`); the rate limit is `public_form_ip` (`RATE_LIMIT_PUBLIC_FORMS`) in the route middleware, counting every post, then `public_form_email` (subject: SHA-256 of the lower-cased email), spent only by a post that passed validation, last in the transaction that stores it: a `400`, a `422` or a failed insert spends nothing (the visitor's corrected post goes through), a denied post rolls its row and event back (concurrent duplicates serialize on the bucket); both forms spend the same buckets; a filled honeypot field `website` is answered `201 {received: true}` like a stored post, spends the email budget like one and stores nothing; a body with trailing data is `400`; waitlist `email` and `name` required, `countryCode` `^\+[0-9]{1,4}$` (default `+66`), `phone` optional; partner interest `companyName`, `contactName`, `email` required, the other `PartnerInterestData` fields optional, stored with `status "new"`; `422 invalid_argument` names every bad field. `GET /v1/waitlist?cursor&limit` (50, at most 500) carries `meta.total`.

### B.2.20 Uploads, files, realtime stream, jobs, operations

| Method | Path | Capability / auth | Listener | Phase | Replaces | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| POST | `/v1/uploads/presign` | authenticated | internal | P0 | every `putData` + `getDownloadURL` site in web (main spec §9) | `{purpose, entityId?, contentType, sizeBytes, sha256?, key?, variant?, fileName?}` -> `200 {key, url, method: "PUT", headers, expiresAt, storageBackend}`; inserts `file_objects (status='pending', purpose, uploaded_by, tenant_id, expires_at, storage_backend)` (R1; `storage_backend` = `STORAGE_BACKEND`, T11); `key?` re-signs a still-pending key of the caller on the row's own backend (another 24 h); `variant` (photo type, document kind, attachment index) and `fileName` feed the key template (main spec §9.2); size <= `UPLOAD_MAX_BYTES`; TTL `S3_PRESIGN_PUT_TTL`; `headers` must be sent as given with a body of exactly `sizeBytes` and its `Content-Length` (`Content-Type` and the length are signed on both backends, `If-None-Match: *` on `s3` too: a second PUT on the URL, also after the commit, is `412`; a chunked body `411`/`403`). `s3`: the URL is signed for `S3_PRESIGN_ENDPOINT` (the browser/APK-reachable `MEDIA_DOMAIN` origin; `S3_ENDPOINT` is server-side only, R74). `local`: `url` is the API path `/v1/uploads/local/{key}?X-LT-Expires&X-LT-Signature`, which the browser sends through the BFF as `/api/go` + `url` (main spec §9.11). `rl:presign_user` 120/min. Errors: `422 invalid_argument` (`purpose`, `contentType`, `sizeBytes`, `sha256`, `variant`, `fileName`, `entityId`, `key` with reason), `403 permission_denied` (`user_photo` of another user), `403 tenant_required` (no active tenant and not `platform_admin`). The entity endpoint that references the key commits it (Stat on its backend, size, type, `status='committed'`) inside its own tx; a failure is `422 invalid_argument` naming the key (main spec §9.4) |
| PUT | `/v1/uploads/local/{key}` | signed URL (no bearer) | internal | P0 | — | Local storage backend only (T11, main spec §9.11): the web's upload through the BFF. HMAC-SHA256 (`LOCAL_MEDIA_SIGNING_KEY`) over method, key, expiry, the `Content-Type` header and `Content-Length` (the declared size); a head that verifies gets the signed size as its body limit (at most `UPLOAD_MAX_BYTES`) and a 5 min read timeout, any other head the 4 MiB / 30 s defaults (`413` above 4 MiB, unread); staged under `.tmp/` and renamed into place (0640) while the pending row is locked, the lock the commit takes; `200` + `ETag`. Missing, tampered or expired signature, another content type or length `403 permission_denied` (`details.reason` `signature_invalid` \| `signature_expired`); the key is not (or no longer) a pending local upload, e.g. committed meanwhile, `409 failed_precondition` (`reason=not_pending`); invalid key or backend not configured `404` |
| GET | `/v1/files` | authenticated (entity authorisation) | internal | P0 | raw Firebase download-token URLs in `<img>` and downloads | `?key=` -> `302` to a short-lived URL on the object's own backend (`S3_PRESIGN_GET_TTL`; `local`: a signed `/media` URL), `Cache-Control: private, no-store`; the web uses it as `/api/go/v1/files?key=`. Readable (T11): public objects, the caller's own uploads (also pending) and, under `X-Act-On-Tenant: *`, everything; then the owner kind's registered authorizer decides, authoritatively ("a file is readable iff its referencing row is"); without one, committed objects of the caller's tenant or of a carrier working for it when the caller is staff (as `p_read`), except that driver ID cards and licences need `drivers:view_pii`, leave evidence `hr:view_leave`, penalty evidence `hr:view_payroll`, statement documents `accounting:billing_result`, and reports and a tenant's ID cards have no staff rule; everything else `404`. URL lifetime `S3_PRESIGN_GET_TTL` capped per purpose: 5 min driver PII and tenant ID cards, 15 min documents, reports, leave and penalty evidence (main spec §9.5) |
| GET | `/media/{key}` | signed URL (no bearer); `app_releases/` unsigned | public | P0 | Firebase download-token URLs | Local storage backend only (T11, main spec §9.11): `${LOCAL_MEDIA_PUBLIC_BASE_URL}/{key}?X-LT-Expires&X-LT-Signature[&response-content-disposition]`, also `HEAD`; streams the file with the content type recorded at upload, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `Cache-Control: private, max-age={seconds left}` (`app_releases/`: `public, max-age=31536000, immutable`). A path ending in `/` `404` (no index); unsigned, tampered or expired `403 permission_denied`; unknown key or backend not configured `404`. On the first deployment nginx maps `https://logi.showkhun.co/media/` to the public listener |
| PUT | `/media/{key}` | signed URL (no bearer) | public | P7a | mobile `putData` sites | Local storage backend only: the driver app's upload (an absolute URL from `POST /v1/mobile/uploads/presign`); same contract as `PUT /v1/uploads/local/{key}` |
| GET | `/v1/events` | authenticated | internal | P0 | 31 web `onSnapshot` listeners (disposition per W7, Appendix E) | SSE stream for the web via the BFF (bearer from cookie, no ticket); `?topics=` explicit topics validated against the principal; implicit topics in §B.4.2; event ids from one global sequence, so `Last-Event-ID` replays across every topic of the connection (R52, §B.4.1); transport ships in P0, topics are added per domain phase |
| GET | `/v1/jobs` | authenticated (own jobs) \| `platform` (all; `support` read-only) | internal | P0 | 540 s callables with client-side timeouts | `?type&cursor&limit` over `jobs` (R14), keyset `(created_at, id)` DESC; any other parameter `400 bad_request`, a bad `type`, `limit` or `cursor` `422 invalid_argument` (T10); `support` reads every job (read-only); no `X-Act-On-Tenant` is needed (jobs has no RLS, R67), so a platform read of jobs is not a `platform_cross_tenant_access`; staff of a job's tenant holding its type's capability (`EffectiveTenant()` = `jobs.tenant_id`, `Can(cap)`) read it too: the type -> capability table `jobs.TypeCapabilities` holds `distances.compute` -> `operations:calculate_distances` since T21 and `bangchak.snapshot` -> `accounting:edit_rate_card` since T37 (a cron fire has no owner and no tenant, so only the platform reads it), and T38, T44 add theirs; a type without a row (`queue.replay`, cron jobs) stays owner + platform (main spec §7.5, T07) |
| GET | `/v1/jobs/{id}` | authenticated (owner) \| `platform` (`support` read-only) | internal | P0 | same | `{id, type, status: queued\|running\|succeeded\|failed, params, progress{done,total}, result?, error?, createdAt, startedAt, finishedAt}` (R64); live updates via SSE `job.updated` on `user:{uid}`; a job the caller may not see is `404 not_found`; `support` reads every job (read-only); tenant staff readers as for `GET /v1/jobs` |
| POST | `/v1/admin/queues/{queue}/replay` | `platform` (`platform_admin`) | internal | P0 | — | `202 {jobId}`: a `queue.replay` job (`params {queue}`, `lock:job:queue.replay:{queue}`, held -> `409 already_exists` with `details.jobId`) and `queue_replayed` in one tx; unknown queue `404`. The leader scheduler runs the job (`api` never connects to the broker): it moves the messages that were in `{queue}.dead` when it started back to the work queue (§B.5.4), result `{moved}` (T10) |

### B.2.21 Driver app group (`/v1/mobile/*`, public listener)

The APK 4.x client (P7a, main spec §11) calls only this group plus `/v1/auth/*` (R42). Every row requires a driver principal unless the auth cell says otherwise: either a Go access token with the `drv` claim (from login, Google sign-in or the one-time `POST /v1/auth/exchange`), or, on the rows marked **shim target**, an `X-Api-Key` of scope `cf_shim` together with the Firebase ID token forwarded by a Cloud Functions shim (§B.2.22, accepted from P2 regardless of `AUTH_FIREBASE_BRIDGE_MODE`, R45). Without a `cf_shim` key, a Firebase ID token is accepted only as `idToken` in the body of `POST /v1/auth/exchange`, and only while `AUTH_FIREBASE_BRIDGE_MODE` includes `mobile` (P7a onwards; Appendix C §C.6.1, §C.6.2); APK 4.x exchanges it once and then uses Go tokens. Anywhere else a Firebase token is `401 invalid_token`. The Phase column is the phase in which the route first ships: shim targets ship with their shim (P2-P5), everything else for APK 4.x in P7a. Rows marked `✱` are replayed by the device's offline outbox. "Same service" names the staff route whose service the handler reuses; the driver handler adds the `driver:self` check and never exposes billing, PII or other drivers' rows. Until P7b the services still write Firestore (write-back mode, main spec §13), so APK 3.x and 4.x coexist.
